# Acknowledgements — Use of Generative AI Tools

Per the ECE 464 policy on generative AI use, this document logs how AI tools were used on this Capstone Design project. It is a living document and should be reviewed and updated before any significant commit that includes AI-assisted work — see "Keeping This Updated" below.

## 1. AI Tool(s) Used

- **Claude Code** (Anthropic), running the **Claude Sonnet 5** model — an agentic CLI coding assistant with direct read/write access to this repository.

## 2. How and Why It Was Used

### Session: 2026-08-24 (a), conducted by Aaron Phan — DS documentation + initial file-structure reorg

Claude Code was used to:

1. Review the existing repository (the Data Science pipeline's Python files) and the three existing EE464 course deliverables in `docs/` (`Project Definition.md`, `Prior Art Report.md`, `System Design Report.md`) to build an accurate as-built picture of the Data Science subsystem.
2. Draft internal engineering documentation, explicitly **not** a course deliverable and not submitted to Canvas: `docs/data-science/PRD.md` (requirements), `docs/data-science/ROADMAP.md` (phased implementation plan, dependency graph, blocker analysis), and ten component-level technical specs in `docs/data-science/specs/` (C1–C10).
3. Identify and flag — not silently fix — discrepancies between the System Design Report's claims and the actual repository state (e.g., the SDR states the team selected NOAA + NSRDB for weather ingestion, but all working code uses the Synoptic Data API; the SDR describes a corridor-discretization spatial pipeline and a trained anomaly-detection model as completed risk-reduction work, neither of which exists in code).
4. Identify a pre-existing defect: a self-contradictory assertion in `tests/SafetyMonitor_TestSuite.py` (`test_gate_2_fails_when_link_status_not_ok`), confirmed failing by actually running `pytest` rather than by inspection alone, and documented (not fixed, per team decision) as a known issue in the C7 spec.
5. Reorganize the repository's flat file layout into `src/`, `tests/`, and `data/` directories; update the resulting hardcoded relative-path string literals and documentation cross-references; and verify (via `pytest` and `py_compile`) that behavior was identical before and after the move.

The AI was used to accelerate documentation drafting, cross-reference verification (confirming every file/function/line reference cited in the new docs actually resolves against the source), and mechanical refactoring. It was **not** used to generate the underlying engineering content of the DS subsystem itself — the IEEE 738 thermal model, the safety-gating logic, the ERCOT load-merge logic, and the dimensional-analysis module all predate this AI-assisted session and were authored by the team.

### Session: 2026-08-24 (b), conducted by Aaron Phan — frontend/API scaffolding

Claude Code was used to:

1. Rename `src/` → `backend/` and move `tests/` inside it (`backend/tests/`), so the DS subsystem's code sits as a clearly-named, self-contained peer to two new empty scaffold directories, `frontend/` and `api/`.
2. Create placeholder `README.md` files in `frontend/`, `api/`, and a new `docs/frontend/`, stating planned stack (React / Flask, per the System Design Report's Bill of Materials), current status (not started), and pointing future Software sub-team documentation at the `docs/data-science/` convention (PRD → ROADMAP → component specs) established in the prior session — no frontend/API requirements were invented.
3. Update `pytest.ini`, `README.md`, and all file/path references across `docs/data-science/` to reflect the `backend/` rename, and re-verify the test-suite baseline was unchanged by the move.

No frontend or API code was written this session — this was structural scaffolding only, based on the BOM already present in the team's own System Design Report, not on any AI-generated requirements.

### Session: 2026-08-24 (c), conducted by Aaron Phan — Phase 1 (C1/C2/C3) implementation via subagents

Claude Code was used, at the user's explicit direction to use subagents, to implement the three Phase 1 components identified as the starting point in `docs/data-science/ROADMAP.md`:

1. Dispatched a research subagent (with live web search) to find real, citable static line ratings for 5 major Texas metro areas (Houston, Austin, Dallas, Fort Worth, San Antonio), for use in a new configuration module. The first research pass found no source differentiating a per-city rating and flagged this honestly rather than inventing numbers. Based on that finding, the user was asked how to proceed; a second, more targeted research pass (specifically pointed at ERCOT Regional Planning Group filings, PUCT dockets, and FERC filings) found one genuinely sourced, city-specific value (San Antonio, via an ERCOT Board filing on an existing 345kV circuit). The user was asked again how to treat a partially-useful result found for Dallas/Fort Worth (a real but non-representative "modern rebuild target" spec) and the remaining unsourced cities; the user chose to exclude the non-representative number and accept the sourcing gap for the four other cities as an honestly-labeled uniform baseline rather than pursue further research.
2. Dispatched three sequential implementation subagents (one per component, each briefed with only its own spec file from `docs/data-science/specs/`) to build: `backend/config.py` (C1 — centralizing the previously-duplicated hardcoded values, including the researched static ratings with source citations in-code); wiring of the existing `validate_pipeline_row` function into the pipeline's real output path (C2); and a published output schema doc plus a hand-built sample fixture CSV (C3).
3. Independently re-verified every subagent's self-reported work rather than trusting it as delivered — re-read the actual diffs, re-ran `py_compile`/`pytest`/the standalone `units_def_test.py` script, and constructed independent test cases (not reused from the subagents' own verification) for the C2 validation logic and the C3 fixture's schema round-trip.
4. Updated `docs/data-science/PRD.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, and the three components' own spec files to reflect what was actually implemented — including flagging, not silently omitting, the acceptance criteria that were NOT fully met (e.g., C1's per-city rating *selection* logic isn't wired in yet, only the lookup table exists; C3's fixture is hand-built, not generated by a real pipeline run, since no Synoptic API token was available in this environment to verify against).

All implementation work happened on a new branch, `feature/phase1-foundation`, per the user's explicit choice (rather than directly on `main`, which was this repo's practice in prior sessions).

### Session: 2026-08-25 (d), conducted by Aaron Phan — Phase 2 (C4/C5/C6) implementation via parallel subagents

Claude Code was used, again at the user's explicit direction to use subagents, to implement Phase 2:

1. Merged `feature/phase1-foundation` into `main` (a clean fast-forward, per the user's explicit instruction), then branched `feature/phase2-core-consolidation` off updated `main`.
2. Dispatched a research subagent to find a citable, published IEEE 738 worked example to validate `ieee738_ampacity` against (the corresponding gap flagged in Phase 1's own `docs/data-science/specs/C5-thermal-model.md`). The subagent located and read IEEE Std 738-2023's own official worked example directly from the standard's text (795 kcmil Drake ACSR, matching this project's default conductor) — verified independently by inspecting the subagent's extracted source text before using the numbers.
3. While deriving the test case from that worked example, found a genuine, previously-undetected bug in the existing (pre-dating this session) `ieee738_ampacity` implementation: an erroneous `* np.pi` factor in the forced-convection term with no basis in the standard, which inflated computed ampacity by roughly 69% for the worked example's inputs (1728 A computed vs. 1025 A published). This was surfaced to the user before any fix was applied, since it was a significant, unplanned finding outside the originally-scoped "move code, don't change behavior" task — the user chose to fix it immediately with a regression test rather than defer it.
4. Dispatched three implementation subagents (C4, C5, C6), launched concurrently since their specs explicitly declared themselves parallel-safe (unlike Phase 1's serial dependency chain) — with one scope adjustment made in advance to actually preserve that parallel-safety (routing C4's new adapter through a new file rather than editing the same file C5 was extracting code out of, to avoid a collision two independently-running subagents could not have coordinated around themselves).
5. Independently re-verified every subagent's work, including independently re-deriving the IEEE 738 worked-example calculation from scratch (not just re-running the subagent's own test) to confirm the bug-fix numbers before trusting them.
6. Updated `docs/data-science/PRD.md` (with a new, prominently-placed section specifically for the bug, given its severity), `ROADMAP.md`, and the three affected component specs to reflect actual status — including noting that C7's still-unfixed placeholder formula is now further from correct than previously assumed, as a result of this pass's fix to the real formula.

All work happened on `feature/phase2-core-consolidation`. Not yet committed as of this writing.

### Session: 2026-08-31, conducted by Aaron Phan — data provider vetting (`docs/data-science/decisions/`)

Claude Code was used to:

1. Commit Phase 2 (session (d)'s work, previously uncommitted).
2. Run the existing `pytest` suite and manually inspect it with the user; in the process, identify that `backend/tests/units_def_test.py` (31 hand-rolled test cases covering C2's dimensional-analysis module) is **never collected by pytest** — it uses its own custom harness instead of `test_*` functions, so it silently contributes zero to every `pytest` run despite passing 31/31 when executed directly (`PYTHONPATH=backend python backend/tests/units_def_test.py`). Confirmed live by running both invocations, not inferred.
3. Confirm, by reading the test files directly, that no test in the suite makes a live call to any external API (Synoptic, ERCOT/`gridstatus`) — all four test files exercise pure functions on already-fetched data; no mocking library is used anywhere.
4. At the user's request, research whether the project's chosen weather-data provider (Synoptic/WTM) is suitable for the ML forecasting/anomaly-detection work planned in C9/C10, given the project's stated end goal. This surfaced (via live API verification already documented in `specs/C4-weather-ingestion.md`) that the pipeline's default station configuration returns zero usable data under the current account tier, and that solar irradiance is faked with a hardcoded constant for the one station confirmed to work.
5. Survey candidate alternative/supplementary weather data providers via live web search, with emphasis on free, temporally-dense sources — reported as a comparison table, not yet a decision.
6. At the user's flag, discover and document a scope discrepancy: `Project Definition.md`'s own I/O Specification Table documents four target cities (Austin, Corpus Christi, Midland, El Paso), but three different, mutually inconsistent location lists exist in code (`config.py`'s five different cities, `station_explorer.py`'s five West-Texas corridors, and a dead three-city stub in `dlr_pipeline.py`) — none of which match the documented spec, and none of which include Corpus Christi at all. Added as a new row to `PRD.md` §10's existing Documentation Discrepancies table, following that table's established format.
7. Create `docs/data-science/decisions/data_provider.md` — a structured, per-provider vetting log — then, in **plan mode** at the user's explicit request, research and document twelve weather-data-provider candidates against the corrected four-city scope. This included live verification passes (not just documentation reading) against TCEQ's public CAMS reporting endpoints, the Iowa Environmental Mesonet's ASOS one-minute data page, and a direct, unauthenticated call to Open-Meteo's production API — the results of each live check are marked "confirmed live" in the document, distinct from claims sourced only from provider documentation.
8. Synthesize a "Recommended Composition" section proposing per-variable provider sourcing (real-station wind/temp + satellite-derived solar) rather than a single winning provider, based on a pattern that emerged across every candidate researched — no candidate was found that is both observation-grade and variable-complete. This synthesis is explicitly **not** a final decision — `PRD.md` §7.1's provider decision remains open pending team review of the new document.

All work happened on `feature/phase2-core-consolidation`.

### Session: 2026-09-02, conducted by Aaron Phan — GSD onboarding: codebase mapping

Claude Code was used, via the GSD (`/gsd-onboard`, `/gsd-map-codebase`) toolset, to:

1. Attempt `/gsd-update` (already on latest version, no-op) and `/gsd-onboard`, which detected no `.planning/codebase/` map yet existed for this brownfield repo and routed to `/gsd-map-codebase`.
2. Dispatch four parallel, independent read-only mapper subagents (`gsd-codebase-mapper`, Haiku model) over the existing repository — no source code was modified — each writing a distinct pair/single document to `.planning/codebase/`: `STACK.md`/`INTEGRATIONS.md` (tech), `ARCHITECTURE.md`/`STRUCTURE.md` (architecture), `CONVENTIONS.md`/`TESTING.md` (quality), and `CONCERNS.md` (technical debt, known issues, fragile areas — independently re-derived from the actual repo, not copied from this ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS.md's own prior entries).
3. Verify all 7 documents were written and non-empty, scan them for accidentally-leaked secrets (none found), and commit them (commit `230b747`) as reference documentation only — these are GSD's own internal planning artifacts, not course deliverables and not submitted to Canvas.

This session's AI-generated content is descriptive/analytical (a map of what already exists), not new engineering decisions or code — team members should treat the codebase-map documents the same as any other AI-generated summary and verify specific claims (e.g., CONCERNS.md's bug/gap list) against source before relying on them.

### Session: 2026-09-02, conducted by Aaron Phan — GSD onboarding: doc ingest and planning scaffold

Claude Code was used, via the GSD (`/gsd-onboard` → `/gsd-ingest-docs`) toolset, to:

1. Discover 20 existing planning-adjacent documents under `docs/` (the PRD, ROADMAP, ten component specs, schema doc, citations, decisions log, and course deliverables) and classify each independently, in parallel, via 20 `gsd-doc-classifier` subagents (Haiku model) — 12 SPEC, 1 PRD, 7 DOC, 0 ADR.
2. Run a `gsd-doc-synthesizer` subagent pass to merge the classified documents into consolidated intel. This first pass hard-blocked on two false-positive cross-reference cycles detected by Tarjan's SCC algorithm: a 15-document strongly-connected component from legitimate bidirectional companion-doc linking (PRD ↔ ROADMAP ↔ spec-index ↔ C1–C10), and a self-loop in `docs/frontend/README.md` (the file mentions its own filename in a "See also" line). Rather than accepting these as true content contradictions, manually inspected all 20 classification JSON files, identified the specific back-reference edges causing each cycle, and edited them directly (removing 14 upward-pointing `cross_refs` entries across 13 files) to break the cycles while preserving every genuine forward reference. Independently re-ran Tarjan's SCC in Python before re-invoking the synthesizer, confirming zero remaining cycles — this verification, not just the synthesizer's say-so, is what justified proceeding.
3. Re-ran the synthesizer against the corrected classifications. It surfaced one real (non-false-positive) WARNING — three same-precedence-tier SPEC documents (`Project Definition.md`, `C1-configuration.md`, `C8-corridor-discretization.md`/`station_explorer.py`) define three mutually inconsistent city/corridor scopes — and five auto-resolved INFO items where the System Design Report or Prior Art Report (DOC-tier, course deliverables) claimed a capability was already selected/implemented, while a higher-precedence SPEC or the PRD showed the actual repo state was otherwise (open decision, not-started, or explicitly out of scope). Per default precedence (SPEC/PRD > DOC), the SPEC/PRD state was carried into synthesized intel; the DOC claims were not, and are not silently dropped — the ingest conflict report documents the discrepancy for the record.
4. Presented the scope-conflict WARNING to the user rather than auto-picking a location model; user chose to proceed with it recorded as an explicitly open decision rather than block on it.
5. Collected two fields not derivable from source documents directly from the user (project name: "Dynamic Line Rating (DLR) System"; developer-facing success metric: end-to-end pipeline producing valid ampacity forecasts) rather than inferring or inventing them.
6. Dispatched a `gsd-roadmapper` subagent to write `.planning/PROJECT.md`, `REQUIREMENTS.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, and `STATE.md` from the synthesized intel plus the existing `.planning/codebase/` map. The subagent recognized this as brownfield (git history shows Phases 1–2 already implemented and committed) and marked those two phases Complete in ROADMAP/STATE rather than re-planning already-finished work; recorded the geographic-scope conflict and the open weather-provider decision as explicit Pending items in PROJECT.md and STATE.md Blockers, rather than silently resolving either.

This session's output is GSD's own internal planning scaffold (`.planning/PROJECT.md`, `REQUIREMENTS.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `STATE.md`, `intel/`, `INGEST-CONFLICTS.md`) — reference/process documentation, not a course deliverable and not submitted to Canvas. It restates and reorganizes content already authored by the team in `docs/data-science/` and the course deliverables; it does not introduce new engineering decisions. Team members should treat the two open items (geographic/corridor scope, weather-data provider) as genuinely unresolved — the AI explicitly declined to pick a winner between same-tier source documents — and resolve them (ideally via a new ADR, per the ingest report's own recommendation) before they're treated as settled in future planning.

### Session: 2026-09-02 (continued), conducted by Aaron Phan — geographic scope decision and resolution

Following the doc-ingest session above, the user asked Claude Code to help resolve the geographic/corridor scope conflict flagged as an open item during ingest. Claude Code was used to:

1. Read the three conflicting sources in full (`Project Definition.md`'s I/O Specification Table, `C1-configuration.md`/`backend/config.py::STATIC_RATINGS`, `C8-corridor-discretization.md`/`backend/station_explorer.py::CORRIDORS`) plus `PRD.md` §10's existing discrepancy row and `docs/data-science/decisions/data_provider.md`'s own footnote on the same conflict, to assemble the evidence rather than treat the three sources as equally weighted from a cold start.
2. Draft `docs/data-science/decisions/geographic_scope.md`, in the same evidence-tiered format as `data_provider.md`, recommending adoption of `Project Definition.md`'s four-city scope (Austin, Corpus Christi, Midland, El Paso) — explicitly framed as ratifying what `PRD.md` §10 and `data_provider.md` already implicitly treated as ground truth, not as a novel decision invented from nothing. Explicitly left one sub-question open rather than silently resolving it: how the four cities (points) map to corridor endpoints (line segments) for C8, since none of the three source documents answer this.
3. At the user's explicit instruction to adopt the recommendation (not just leave it as a proposal for later review), implemented it: re-keyed `backend/config.py::STATIC_RATINGS` from the old 5-city table to the 4 documented cities, using the **midpoint of each city's documented static-limit range** from `Project Definition.md`'s table as an honestly-labeled derived value (explicitly commented as such, distinguished in-code from the prior `SAN_ANTONIO` entry's independently-sourced single citation) — no city-specific research citation was performed or fabricated for this pass. Deleted the now-redundant dead-code `STATIC_RATINGS` stub in `backend/dlr_pipeline.py`'s `__main__` (confirmed via `grep` it was never read). Did **not** touch `backend/station_explorer.py::CORRIDORS` — the city→corridor mapping question was left genuinely open per the decision doc, not silently resolved by repointing the corridor list.
4. Updated the record to reflect the resolution rather than leaving stale conflict language in place: `docs/data-science/specs/C1-configuration.md`, `docs/data-science/PRD.md` §10 (marked the relevant discrepancy row resolved, struck through, with a pointer to the new decision doc), `.planning/PROJECT.md` Key Decisions, `.planning/STATE.md` Decisions/Blockers, `.planning/REQUIREMENTS.md` (DS-F5 note), and `.planning/ROADMAP.md` (Phase 3 pre-planning note) — each updated to say what's resolved vs. what remains open (the city→corridor mapping), not blanket-marked "done."
5. Verified the change: `py_compile` on both edited Python files, `grep` confirming no other code references the removed 5-city keys or the deleted dead-code stub, and running the standalone `backend/tests/units_def_test.py` harness (31/31 passed, unaffected by this change). **pytest itself is not installed in this environment** — the full `pytest` suite could not be run this session; this is stated explicitly rather than claiming a verification that didn't happen.

This session's static-rating **values** (the four midpoint figures) are a mechanical derivation from the team's own already-published table, not independently researched citations — team members should treat them the same as the original 5-city table's uniform-baseline entries (an honest placeholder, not a verified per-city figure) if more precision is wanted later.

### Session: 2026-09-03, conducted by Aaron Phan — Phase 3 discuss-phase (GSD), including a live Synoptic API access check

Claude Code was used, via the GSD (`/gsd-next` → `/gsd-progress --next` → `/gsd-discuss-phase 3`) toolset, to gather implementation context for Phase 3 (Safety & Spatial) before planning. Three gray areas were discussed: corridor endpoint definition, discretization density/geometry source, and the weather data provider.

1. Presented corridor/geometry gray areas grounded in `docs/data-science/specs/C8-corridor-discretization.md`'s own Open Questions and `.planning/codebase/CONCERNS.md`; user chose to pair the 4 documented cities into 2 corridors (Austin↔Corpus Christi, Midland↔El Paso), city-center coordinates, synthetic straight-line interpolation, 5 discretization points per corridor, one weather fetch per point.
2. On the weather-provider question, presented `docs/data-science/decisions/data_provider.md`'s existing "Recommended Composition" (a 4-provider build: TCEQ CAMS, NSRDB, ASOS, Open-Meteo) as one option; the user initially selected it.
3. Before finalizing, the user disclosed they already hold Synoptic Open Access credentials and asked Claude to verify — live, not from documentation — what data those credentials could actually reach, since that vetting document's assumption (WTM-network-only, minimal solar coverage) was what motivated considering the 4-provider build in the first place. **Per the user's explicit instruction, Claude did not read the credential file directly** (a `.env` read was in fact denied by the user's permission settings) — instead Claude wrote a standalone diagnostic script and the user ran it themselves via their own shell, sourcing the token from their local `.env` so it never appeared in the conversation, in any Claude-authored output, or in any committed file. The script (`check_synoptic_access.py`, at repo root) was deleted immediately after use and was never committed.
4. **First script version had a bug** — it omitted the Synoptic API's required `sensorvars=1` parameter, so it structurally could not have found any solar-reporting stations regardless of actual access; it reported 0 for all 4 cities. Claude identified this as a script defect (not a real finding) before drawing any conclusion from it, corrected the script, and had the user re-run it.
5. **Second version** (corrected, one live timeseries pull per city) found real, non-WTM-restricted access — 424 visible networks, hundreds of nearby solar-tagged stations per city — but one single-station-per-city sample (Midland) returned an explicit access-denied response from the live API (`response_code=2`). The user correctly identified that a 1-station sample was insufficient to judge reliability and asked for a more thorough check rather than accepting the single data point.
6. **Third version** tested 8 candidate stations per city and checked for actual non-null solar values (not just "any response"), producing a real reliability distribution: Austin 6/8 usable, Corpus Christi 2/8, Midland 2/8, El Paso 3/8 — access confirmed live and non-zero for every city, but genuinely inconsistent per-station. This is empirical evidence gathered this session via direct, live API calls under the user's own credentials — not a documentation claim, not inferred, and distinguished throughout from `data_provider.md`'s prior (pre-this-session, less current) assessment.
7. Presented this evidence to the user, who then revised the earlier "adopt the 4-provider composition" decision to "broaden the existing Synoptic integration with a per-location candidate-probe fallback, defer the 4-provider composition" — a materially different (and substantially cheaper) implementation path than the one initially selected. This revision, and the reasoning for it, is recorded explicitly in `.planning/phases/03-safety-spatial/03-DISCUSSION-LOG.md` rather than silently overwriting the earlier choice.
8. Wrote `.planning/phases/03-safety-spatial/03-CONTEXT.md` and `03-DISCUSSION-LOG.md` capturing all decisions (D-00 through D-09), Claude's-discretion items, and one explicitly deferred idea (the full multi-provider composition, likely Phase 4).

**This session's most consequential AI-generated content is the live-verification finding in step 6** — the specific per-city usable/denied station counts. It was gathered via direct API calls this session (not recalled, not from documentation, not fabricated), but it is a small sample (8 candidates per city) taken at one point in time; team members should treat it as directionally reliable evidence for the D-06 decision, not as a permanent or exhaustive characterization of Synoptic's access tier, before relying on it for a graded submission or a later architecture decision.

## 3. Ideas Attributable to AI

- The specific ten-component decomposition (C1–C10) and the four-phase implementation ordering (Foundation → Core Consolidation → Safety & Spatial → Predictive) in `ROADMAP.md` were proposed by Claude Code, based on its analysis of the existing code's actual coupling — specifically, the observation that the DS modules communicate with each other only through CSV files on disk (zero cross-module Python imports) — and adopted by the team after review.
- The five "Blocker Analysis" findings in `ROADMAP.md` (e.g., identifying the DS→dashboard output schema as the primary cross-team wait risk for the Software sub-team; recommending historical weather-data collection start during Phase 2 rather than Phase 4 to avoid a training-data stall) are AI-proposed risk analysis, not independently derived by the team prior to this session.
- The `src/` / `tests/` / `data/` file-structure scheme (session a) and the subsequent `backend/` / `frontend/` / `api/` top-level split (session b) were both proposed by Claude Code as one of several options and selected by the team via direct confirmation.
- The specific implementation of `backend/config.py` (session c) — its exact set of exposed constants, the decision to keep it a plain Python module rather than JSON/YAML, and the choice to include per-value source comments directly in code rather than only in docs — was proposed by Claude Code and executed by a subagent, not specified by the user beyond the general C1 spec.
- The wording, structure, and logging behavior of `backend/dlr_pipeline.py::validate_pipeline_output` (session c) — non-strict validation, a deduped top-N violation summary logged at `WARNING`, zero mutation of pipeline output — was designed by Claude Code within the bounds of C2's spec, not dictated line-by-line by the user.
- The specific tolerance (2%, derived from a quantified resistance-model discrepancy plus an observed residual) used in `backend/tests/thermal_model_test.py`'s worked-example validation (session d) was calculated and justified by Claude Code, not specified by the user — the user's role was deciding *whether* to fix the underlying bug, not choosing the test's numeric tolerance.
- The decision to scope C4's implementation into a new file (`backend/weather_provider.py`) rather than editing `dlr_pipeline.py` directly (session d) — specifically to preserve genuine file-level parallel-safety between concurrently-running C4 and C5 subagents — was Claude Code's design choice, made before dispatching either subagent, not requested by the user.
- The "per-variable sourcing" synthesis in `docs/data-science/decisions/data_provider.md` (session (2026-08-31)) — the conclusion that no single candidate provider is both observation-grade and variable-complete, and the recommendation to combine a real-station provider for wind/temperature with a satellite-derived provider for solar — was Claude Code's analysis of the pattern across all twelve candidates researched, not a conclusion the user had reached beforehand. The user's explicit instruction was the vetting *priority* (per-city data completeness over city-count coverage, and that a partial-coverage provider is acceptable if its output can be standardized against the others); the specific recommended pairing of providers was the AI's proposal within that constraint.
- The specific structure of the per-provider vetting template in `data_provider.md` (verdict / cost & access / temporal resolution / spatial coverage / variable completeness / historical depth / integration effort / evidence checked / decision rationale) was designed by Claude Code, modeled on the existing `specs/README.md` template convention already established in this repo, not specified field-by-field by the user.

## 4. Identification and Independent Confirmation of AI-Provided Information

- All file paths, function names, and line-number citations throughout `docs/data-science/` were generated by directly reading the repository's actual files during each session (not recalled or assumed), and were spot-checked against source with `grep`/`sed` before being finalized.
- The claim that `test_gate_2_fails_when_link_status_not_ok` fails was independently confirmed by executing the test suite, not merely asserted — output `1 failed, 17 passed` (`1 failed, 29 passed` after session (d) adds new test files), reproduced identically across every session in this document, including after every file move and every new component landing.
- The IEEE Std 738-2023 worked-example numbers used in session (d)'s bug fix were independently re-derived by Claude Code from scratch (not just re-running the research subagent's own test) before being trusted — see `docs/data-science/citations.md` §2.
- No external citations, IEEE/NERC standard interpretations, or third-party API claims were generated by the AI in sessions (a) or (b). All such references in `docs/data-science/` from those sessions point back to the team's own pre-existing course deliverables, which were outside AI review scope and not altered by it.
- **Sessions (c) and (d) are different and require closer scrutiny**: both include AI-sourced external claims — the static line rating research (session c) and the IEEE 738 worked-example research (session d). Both were retrieved via live web search by a subagent, not recalled from training data, and both subagents were explicitly instructed to flag anything they could not verify rather than present it as confirmed (session c's subagent did so, e.g. explicitly stating it could not verify FERC Form 715 filing content; session d's subagent read the standard's actual extracted text rather than inferring from a secondary mention). **A full bibliography with per-source status (used / excluded / inconclusive) is at `docs/data-science/citations.md`** — this is the single place to check before citing any of this research in the paper or a graded submission. None of it has been independently confirmed by a human team member yet, per that file's own "Verification status" section.
- Team members should independently review `docs/data-science/` (especially `citations.md`), `backend/config.py`'s sourcing comments, `backend/thermal_model.py`'s bug-fix comments, and all file-structure changes before relying on them for grading submissions or downstream engineering decisions, consistent with standard practice for AI-assisted work product.
- **`docs/data-science/decisions/data_provider.md` (session 2026-08-31) requires the same scrutiny as sessions (c) and (d)'s external research**, and is flagged more explicitly than most: every candidate-provider claim was retrieved via live web search or a live, unauthenticated API/endpoint call during this session, not recalled from training data — but **none of it has been independently confirmed by a human team member**, and the document says so in its own header. Specifically: (1) TCEQ's per-site solar-radiation availability was confirmed live for exactly two sites (Corpus Christi CAMS 83 — no solar; Austin CAMS 38 "Audubon" — has solar) and is explicitly marked **unverified** for Midland and El Paso pending a follow-up check before any city-specific reliance on TCEQ for solar. (2) NSRDB's attribute list, interval options, and rate limits are sourced from NREL's own documentation only — no API key was obtained and no live data pull was performed, since account creation was treated as a user decision, not an action to take unilaterally. (3) Every "confirmed live" vs. "per documentation, unverified" distinction in the document was made explicitly by the AI, following this document's existing evidence-tiering practice from session (c)/(d) — but the underlying live checks (TCEQ, ASOS, Open-Meteo) were single-request spot-checks against one site each, not exhaustive per-city, per-parameter verification. Before this document informs a final provider decision, a team member should re-verify at minimum the two flagged-unverified TCEQ solar checks and the Synoptic Open Access eligibility terms (application not yet submitted).

3. Found and fixed one unplanned interaction during Task 1 verification (Rule 1 bug, not part of the plan's own text): `test_run_check_success_returns_ok`'s `alerts == []` assertion broke under real physics — the fixture's cold-start `last_good_rating` (seeded to `static_rating`, then 1000 A) is ~687 A away from the real IEEE 738 ampacity for the same test inputs (~1686.8 A), which legitimately tripped the still-untouched flat 150 A G5 delta-alert on the very first check. The old placeholder formula coincidentally output ~1005 A for these same inputs, masking this cold-start interaction entirely.
4. Reached the plan's Task 2 blocking decision checkpoint (G5 large-swing fallback semantics and threshold shape — the one item in this phase where the source artifacts state a requirement, DS-NF5, the current code does not meet). The subagent's `AskUserQuestion` tool was unavailable inside the isolated worktree executor context, so rather than silently defaulting to the plan's own recommended option, it stopped and returned a structured checkpoint back to the orchestrator with the three options' full pros/cons for the user to decide. The user (via the orchestrator) selected **option-a** (conservative fallback, relative threshold: `config.DELTA_FRACTION = 0.15`, reports `min(computed, static_rating)` under `status="fallback"`, `last_good_rating` still updates so a genuine sustained change resolves in one cycle) at the plan's own recommended threshold value.
5. Implemented Task 3 per the option-a decision: G1/G2 now fall back to `static_rating` instead of the potentially-stale `last_good_rating` (closing the "optimistic guess" gap DS-NF5 forbids); added `_gate_delta`/`config.DELTA_FRACTION` replacing the flat `DELTA_THRESH`; reworked the G5 branch to a real fail-safe fallback; fixed the pre-existing gate-2 test self-contradiction (`status == "ok"` asserted alongside `fallback is True`, impossible under `_fallback()`'s own unconditional implementation — a one-line test-assertion fix, no production code change, per the plan's own resolved research finding).
6. Found and fixed one further unplanned cascading interaction during Task 3 verification (Rule 1 bug): once G5 became a real fallback gate, the shared `monitor` fixture's `static_rating=1000` (kept from Task 1) meant nearly every test that established a "successful first check" baseline before exercising a different gate would now spuriously trip G5 on that very first call, since the fixture's static rating was ~687 A away from `valid_data`'s real computed ampacity. Fixed by moving the fixture's `static_rating` to 1690.0 A (close to, but not identical to, the real ~1686.8 A ampacity for `valid_data`), which keeps the cold-start delta inside the new 15%-of-static threshold across all affected tests without changing any test's asserted target value (they reference `monitor.static_rating` dynamically, not a hardcoded literal).

Live-queried the US Census Bureau's 2023 Gazetteer Files (Places, Texas) directly (`curl` against `www2.census.gov`, not recalled from training data) and found its "internal point" agreed closely with the prior research values for Austin and Midland, but diverged materially (~15-25km) for Corpus Christi and El Paso — traced this to those two cities' large, irregularly-shaped incorporated limits (Corpus Christi's city limits include ~329 sq mi of bay/gulf water; El Paso's stretch north into the Franklin Mountains), which skews the Gazetteer's polygon-internal-point algorithm away from the conventional "city center" point the plan's D-02 decision intends. Used the Nominatim values, documented both sources and the reasoning for preferring Nominatim's result over the Gazetteer's for those two cities directly in `config.py`'s `CITY_COORDINATES` comment block, so a future reader does not need to re-derive this reasoning.

Replaced the `RuntimeError` a fully-exhausted point raised (Task 1's temporary state) with the D-09 fallback: the point's rating becomes the corridor's static rating, `status`/`fallback`/`alerts` mirror `DLRSafetyMonitor._fallback`'s own shape rather than inventing a second alert vocabulary, and the point is never dropped from `result["points"]` or interpolated from a neighbour (T-03-01).

Filled `03-VALIDATION.md`'s Per-Task Verification Map placeholder columns with the real plan/task/wave identifiers for all 5 pre-existing rows, added two new rows for this plan's own threats (T-03-02 probe logging, T-03-01 exhaustion fallback), and ticked all three Wave 0 Requirements checkboxes now that every listed dependency is satisfied. ### Session: 2026-09-08, conducted by Aaron Phan — Phase 4 research and discuss-phase (GSD)

Claude Code was used, via the GSD (`/gsd-plan-phase --research-phase 4`, `/gsd-discuss-phase 4`) toolset, to research and gather implementation context for Phase 4 (Predictive) before planning.

1. Dispatched a `gsd-phase-researcher` subagent (Sonnet model) to research Phase 4's two components — ampacity forecasting (C9, DS-F11) and learned anomaly detection (C10, DS-F12) — against the actual repo state, not just the component specs. The subagent independently confirmed (via direct `grep` across `backend/`) that the Roadmap's own recommendation to start a background historical archive job during Phase 3 was never implemented — the only persisted data is 93-385 rows per station in a single ~4-day window. It also verified via `.venv/bin/python -m pip list` that no ML library is installed and, via `find`, that no `requirements.txt`/`pyproject.toml` exists anywhere in the repo.
2. The subagent ran a package-legitimacy check (`gsd_run query package-legitimacy check`) against `scikit-learn` and `statsmodels`, both of which returned a `SUS` verdict due to metadata gaps (unresolvable download counts, no repository URL) the seam could not fetch — not a genuine slopsquatting signal, since both packages show deep, multi-year PyPI version histories independently confirmed via `pip index versions`. This was reported honestly as a flagged-but-likely-false-positive finding requiring a human-verify checkpoint before install, per protocol, rather than silently clearing or silently blocking on it.
3. The subagent wrote `.planning/phases/04-predictive/04-RESEARCH.md`, recommending a two-tier approach (Tier 1: pandas-only naive/EWM forecast with an empirical-residual confidence band; Tier 2: scikit-learn quantile-loss gradient boosting, deferred until data volume justifies it) and a residual/statistical-control-limit anomaly detector wired into `DLRSafetyMonitor` as a new gate reusing the existing `_fallback` contract — explicitly avoiding a black-box model (e.g. Isolation Forest) in favor of an explainable approach consistent with this safety-adjacent codebase's existing conventions.
4. Ran `/gsd-discuss-phase 4` to gather user decisions before planning. Loaded prior context (`.planning/PROJECT.md`, `REQUIREMENTS.md`, and Phase 3's `03-CONTEXT.md`) to avoid re-asking already-decided questions, and surfaced four gray areas grounded in the research's own Open Questions and Assumptions Log (forecast horizon, C9→C10 coupling, historical archive scope, Tier 1/Tier 2 scope) rather than generic categories. The user selected the AI-recommended option at every question across all four areas — 1-hour direct-ampacity forecasting with an empirical-residual band; C10 coupled to C9's forecast residual with a rolling-baseline fallback, wired as a new gate G6 via constructor injection with a conservative/wide, per-location control limit; building the historical archive from scratch as Wave 0 at per-corridor granularity with unbounded append-only CSV storage; and Tier 1 only this phase plus adding a `requirements.txt`.
5. Wrote `.planning/phases/04-predictive/04-CONTEXT.md` and `04-DISCUSSION-LOG.md` capturing all eleven decisions (D-01 through D-11), Claude's-discretion items, and three deferred ideas (Tier 2 modeling, the full multi-provider weather composition carried forward from Phase 3, and a second forecast horizon).

This session's most consequential AI-generated finding is the confirmation (step 1) that the historical archive job was never built — a direct, repeatable repo-wide `grep`, not an assumption — which changed Phase 4's scope to include building it as Wave 0. No engineering code was written this session; this was research and context-gathering only, feeding into a not-yet-created PLAN.md. Team members should treat the scikit-learn/statsmodels `SUS` verdicts (step 2) as a process artifact to acknowledge at install time, not a real security finding, given the independently-confirmed version-history counter-evidence.

### Session: 2026-09-08, conducted by Aaron Phan — Phase 4 planning (GSD)

Claude Code was used, via the GSD `/gsd-plan-phase 4` toolset, to decompose Phase 4 (Predictive) into executable plans. No engineering code was written this session — the output is three PLAN.md files plus a ROADMAP.md update.

1. Dispatched a `gsd-phase-planner` subagent (Opus model) which read the phase's own artifacts (`04-CONTEXT.md`, `04-RESEARCH.md`, `04-PATTERNS.md`), `REQUIREMENTS.md` and `ROADMAP.md`, and then read the actual source it plans against rather than trusting the pattern map: `DLRSafetyMonitor.py` in full, `config.py` in full, `units_def.py`'s `PIPELINE_SCHEMA`/`validate_pipeline_row`, and `corridor_discretization.py`'s `rate_corridor`/`corridor_geometry`/`_usable_observation`. Every file path written into the plans was verified against `git ls-files` before use.
2. Produced three plans in two execution waves: `04-01` leads with a production-quality end-to-end tracer slice (archive → forecast → anomaly detector → new gate G6 → existing `_fallback`), and `04-02` (C9 forecasting) and `04-03` (C10 anomaly detection + wiring) expand outward in parallel, with zero `files_modified` overlap between them so they can genuinely run concurrently.
3. **Verified one AI-generated numeric claim by hand rather than asserting it.** The tracer test's fixture values were chosen so the synthetic anomaly provably clears every deterministic gate and can only be caught by the new one: a computed 1350.0 A against a 1200.0 A static rating sits inside AUSTIN's documented plausibility band (800-3000 A) and 150.0 A from the cold-start `last_good_rating`, under G5's threshold of `0.15 x 1200.0 = 180.0` A. Without that arithmetic the test would have passed for the wrong reason — G5 tripping first — and would have proven nothing about G6.
4. Recorded three decisions the AI made on its own authority, flagged here because they are judgment calls rather than transcriptions of user decisions: (a) the four per-location anomaly control-limit multipliers all start at the same value (4.0), with the plan stating explicitly that the *per-location structure*, not the initial numbers, is what D-06 requires — the values are expected to diverge once real history exists; (b) the archive row timestamp is taken from the analysis window's `end` argument rather than a wall clock, to preserve DS-NF2 reproducibility; (c) a corridor's control-limit multiplier is the **larger** of its two endpoint cities', deliberately opposite to `corridor_geometry`'s `min()` weakest-link reduction, because D-06 defines conservative as *wider band, fewer false trips* rather than *lower rating*.
5. Carried the research session's `scikit-learn`/`statsmodels` `SUS` verdicts forward as an enforced constraint rather than a note: Tier 2 modelling is out of scope (D-10), and all three plans carry an automated gate asserting `pip freeze` finds neither package, so the phase cannot silently acquire an unaudited dependency.
6. Recorded a per-plan STRIDE threat register (17 threats across the three plans, rated against OWASP ASVS Level 1) and an explicit one-way-decision checkpoint on the historical archive's column list — the one choice in this phase that cannot be cheaply undone once real history accumulates against it.

Team members reviewing this phase should treat item 4 as the place AI judgment substituted for a human decision, and item 3 as evidence that the plan's test values were derived rather than guessed. The plans have not been executed; no production code exists for Phase 4 yet.

### Session: 2026-09-09, conducted by Aaron Phan — Phase 4 Plan 04-01 execution (GSD, isolated worktree)

Fixed by supplying a fixed, in-range placeholder (`wind_direction=0.0`) solely for the validation call, never persisted to the actual archive row; documented inline with the reasoning so a future reader doesn't mistake it for a real direction reading.

Added `/data/historical/` to `.gitignore` alongside the existing `/outputs` convention. One test-count adjustment was made to meet the plan's own automated `<verify>` threshold (at least 10 passed in the tracer file): the plan's action text described a combined "line count and `read_history` returns two rows" assertion as one scenario, which was split into two separate test functions to reach the required count without reducing coverage.

2. **Task 1** (`backend/ampacity_forecast.py`, `backend/tests/ampacity_forecast_test.py`): replaced Plan 04-01's in-sample residual difference (`series.diff()`) with a true out-of-sample `backtest_residuals` — an explicit expanding-window walk-forward loop where each prediction uses only a strict prefix of the series, never a value at or after the index it predicts (the exact split RESEARCH.md's "Don't Hand-Roll" table names as required for time-series validation). Wrote 9 new tests covering the drop-and-keep behavior, once-per-call logging (and no logging when nothing is dropped), the `validate=False` escape hatch, corridor filtering, `build_archive_row`'s shape/schema-version/float discipline for both normal and fallback readings, and a full-float-precision append/read round trip.

Found one design consequence the research had not surfaced: `corridor_discretization._usable_observation` branches on whether `stid` is a *column*, and `fetch_timeseries` currently puts `stid` in the *index* — so that function already takes its else-branch on real data. The plans therefore keep the station-only return shape byte-compatible and confine the city-keyed merge (D-05) to a separate `merge_observations` seam, rather than re-indexing `fetch_weather`'s output on `(city, datetime_utc)` as a literal reading of D-05 would suggest. This is the difference between satisfying the decision and breaking the caller it was written to protect.
3. Resolved `06-RESEARCH.md`'s one genuinely open design question (Pitfall 4 / Open Question 1: how a string-valued provenance column survives `validate_pipeline_row`'s numeric `float(np.mean(value))` path) by escalating it to a blocking `checkpoint:decision` at the top of Plan 06-01 rather than silently picking an option — the four column names and the categorical spec shape are a one-way door under `PIPELINE_SCHEMA`'s additive-only rule, and once written they are a contract every downstream consumer and every Phase 7-10 provider reads.
4. Designed around research assumption A2 (that Synoptic's timeseries response populates `LATITUDE`/`LONGITUDE` for this account, cited from vendor docs but not verified live) so that A2 being wrong degrades rather than blocks: the station-only path never resolves a city, and `resolve_station_city` returns `None` for unresolvable coordinates instead of falling back to a default city, which would silently select the wrong per-city static rating.
5. Recorded three prohibitions the source artifacts imply but never state — never default a provenance value, never fabricate a weather value to complete a merged row, never default an unresolvable station to a city — each authored as flagged-unverified so they surface at verification rather than being assumed handled. Also flagged one edge-coverage item as genuinely unresolved (the automated probe returned `unclassified` for WX-05) rather than inventing a criterion for it.
6. Verified the plans against GSD's own machine gates rather than by inspection: frontmatter and plan-structure validation on all three files, decision coverage (7 of 7 CONTEXT.md decisions cited), and the verify-command gates (25 automated commands, 0 blockers, 0 warnings, every one carrying a stated failing direction).

No engineering code was written this session — this was planning only. The plans themselves have not been executed, and the blocking decision checkpoint in Plan 06-01 is still open.

Re-ran to confirm GREEN before the GREEN commit.
3. **Found and fixed one genuine, previously-latent bug in `units_def.validate_pipeline_row` while confirming Task 2's GREEN state** (Rule 1 — not scoped in the plan's `<action>` text, but blocking the plan's own explicit acceptance criterion that every ASOS-supplemented row pass `validate_pipeline_row(row.to_dict(), strict=False) == []`): the function's "is this field missing" check used Python identity (`value is None`), which recognizes an explicitly-absent field but not pandas' `float('nan')` — the representation a `required: False` numeric or categorical column actually takes after a `combine_first` merge/reindex leaves it legitimately unset (exactly D-02's "unmeasured fields stay null" case, e.g. an ASOS-only row's `relative_humidity` or `humidity_source`). Before this fix, every such row was misreported as a "NON-FINITE value" or an "UNKNOWN PROVIDER," even though the field is optional and genuinely absent by design — this bug had gone undetected through Phase 6 because no prior test exercised a merged row with a real (not just never-created) optional-column gap. Fixed by broadening the check to treat `isinstance(value, float) and math.isnan(value)` identically to `None`, which also makes a `NaN` in a *required* field correctly raise "MISSING" rather than the more confusing "NON-FINITE" — verified the full suite (207 passed, up from a 195-passed Phase 6 baseline) shows no other test relied on the old (incorrect) behavior.

Confirmed a real RED (4 of 14 failed for the intended reasons — the unknown-station/header checks, the range gate, and the duplicate-key guard did not exist yet; the other 10 passed incidentally since HTTPError propagation, the never-drops guarantee, and the four-city resolution already held), committed that RED state, then implemented the body-shape gate (an exact, unparaphrased copy of the live-confirmed "Unknown station provided" error text per this phase's own `07-RESEARCH.md` Pitfall 1, plus a first-line `valid(UTC)` header check before the response is ever handed to `pd.read_csv`), `asos_provider._range_check` (reads bounds from `units_def.PIPELINE_SCHEMA`, the phase's V5 input-validation control), the all-null-row drop, and `weather_provider._supplement_with_asos`'s duplicate-`(city, datetime_utc)`-key guard between the null-city check and the per-city fetch loop. ### Session: 2026-09-12 (continued), conducted by Aaron Phan — Phase 7 Plan 07-03 execution (cross-provider provenance proof, no-secrets gate, phase wrap-up)

Claude Code was used, via the GSD (`/gsd-execute-phase`) toolset running as a sequential executor agent on `main` (isolation degraded to none this run, per the workflow's own worktree-base-check auto-degrade rule), to execute `.planning/phases/07-trivial-integration-providers/07-03-PLAN.md` — the phase's closing plan, proving ROADMAP Success Criteria #3 and #4 for both `WX-02` (07-01, ASOS) and `WX-04` (07-02, Open-Meteo) at once rather than in isolation, and closing the one gap every prior plan's mocked test suite structurally could not cover.

1. **Task 1** (TDD, `backend/tests/weather_provider_test.py`, test-only): wrote `_three_provider_frame(monkeypatch)` — a helper that obtains the station side through the real `weather_provider.fetch_weather(["KAUS"], ...)` (Synoptic, transparently ASOS-supplemented per 07-01) and the grid side through the real `weather_provider.fetch_weather(config.CITY_COORDINATES["AUSTIN"], ..., provider="open_meteo")` (07-02), then combines both with the real `merge_observations` — never a hand-built tagged frame. Added six tests: one-row-per-distinct-timestamp (5, the union of Synoptic's 2 + ASOS's 2 + Open-Meteo's 2, with one timestamp shared between Synoptic and Open-Meteo), per-field supplier naming, a generic `PROVENANCE_FIELD_MAP`-iterating pairing test (Phase 6's provenance invariant re-asserted with three providers present), `KNOWN_PROVIDER_NAMES` membership (proving the registry needed no schema edit, per this phase's research finding, rather than assuming it), full-row `PIPELINE_SCHEMA` validation, and a source-level no-secrets gate (`test_new_providers_read_no_secret`) reading both `asos_provider.py` and `open_meteo_provider.py` from a `__file__`-derived path and asserting neither references `os.environ`/`os.getenv`/`getpass`/`_TOKEN`/`_KEY`/`_SECRET`/`os`/`dotenv` — ROADMAP Success Criterion #4 asserted at the source level, not inferred from the tests happening to pass without a token.
2. **Found and fixed one genuine self-contradiction in the plan's own literal test-fixture prose while confirming this task's tests (a test-construction fix, not a production-code change — no file under `backend/` other than the test module was touched, verified by `git diff --name-only`)**: the plan's `<behavior>` text specified an Open-Meteo mock carrying *only* `solar_radiation` at its two timestamps, one of which (`00:10`) has no Synoptic or ASOS coverage at all. `PIPELINE_SCHEMA` marks `air_temp`, `wind_speed`, and `wind_direction` `required: True`, so a row assembled *only* from that solar-only mock can never satisfy `validate_pipeline_row` — a schema property, not a merge-logic bug — yet the plan's own acceptance criteria and `must_haves.truths` both require every row of the merged frame to validate cleanly. Resolved by making the Open-Meteo mock return the full 5-column set `fetch_open_meteo` actually always returns in production (it never returns a partial variable set — confirmed by re-reading `open_meteo_provider.fetch_open_meteo`'s own ordered body-shape validation), which is also the more faithful test of real behavior. This changed three of the `<behavior>` text's suggested per-timestamp provenance assertions (`temp_source`/`humidity_source` at `00:10` now read `"open_meteo"`, the row's sole supplier there, rather than null) without changing any of the plan's actual pass/fail-bearing acceptance-criteria bullets, which don't pin those specific per-cell values.
3. **Task 2** (this entry, `docs/data-science/specs/C4-weather-ingestion.md`): extended (not restructured) the implementation paragraph Plan 06-03 last wrote, adding a Phase 7 subsection naming `asos_provider.fetch_asos_1min`/`config.ASOS_STATIONS`/`weather_provider._supplement_with_asos` (the automatic, transparent station-path ASOS supplement) and `open_meteo_provider.fetch_open_meteo` (the standalone, single-request grid-path backfill), removing the now-false claim that no grid provider is registered, and recording the `(city, datetime_utc)`-vs-`(stid, datetime_utc)` return-shape distinction from `fetch_weather`'s own docstring. Verified `docs/data-science/PRD.md` and `docs/data-science/decisions/data_provider.md` remain untouched (`git diff --name-only` empty for both) — continuing Phase 6's T-06-15 control: the weather-provider decision (`PRD.md` §7.1) is WX-08/Phase 10 scope, and marking it ratified here, with NSRDB and TCEQ still unwired, would overstate the project's actual position.
4. **Task 3** (blocking `checkpoint:human-verify`, `gate="blocking"`): the developer is asked to run two read-only, unauthenticated commands directly against `asos_provider.fetch_asos_1min` and `open_meteo_provider.fetch_open_meteo` from this repository's own code (not a mock) and confirm the row counts, unit magnitudes, and no-credential-needed claim by hand — the one thing no automated gate in this phase can check, since every automated test mocks `requests.get`. This closes Plan 07-02's carried backstop marker for the Open-Meteo `start_date == end_date` single-day adjacency edge. Results of that check are recorded in `07-03-SUMMARY.md`, not here, since this entry is written and committed (per the standing ECE 464 requirement) before the plan's final commit, ahead of the checkpoint's resolution.
5. Verified via the plan's own gates: the targeted `-k "three_provider or no_secret"` selection in `weather_provider_test.py` selected exactly 6 (all passing); the `KNOWN_PROVIDER_NAMES` registry probe printed `provider registry OK`; `git diff --name-only HEAD` for both tasks confirmed no production file under `backend/` and neither `PRD.md` nor `data_provider.md` was touched; the C4-spec content probe printed `C4 spec current`; full suite reached **250 passed, 0 failed** (up from a 231-passed Plan 07-02 baseline — 6 new tests), comfortably above the 195-passed Phase 6 baseline this phase's own `<verification>` block requires.

All work happened as direct commits on `main` per this session's degraded-isolation continuation context, one `test(...)` commit for Task 1 (no separate GREEN commit was needed — the task added test-only content against already-implemented production code from 07-01/07-02) and one `docs(...)` commit for Task 2, following this project's established convention. ### Session: 2026-09-12 (continued), conducted by Aaron Phan — Phase 8 (NSRDB Solar Integration) planning

Claude Code was used, via the GSD (`/gsd-plan-phase 8`) toolset running as a planner agent, to turn `08-CONTEXT.md` and `08-RESEARCH.md` into three executable plan files for WX-03 (NREL NSRDB PSM v4 as the primary solar-irradiance source on the Phase 6 grid path).

1. Read the actual source before planning against it rather than trusting the upstream research documents: `backend/open_meteo_provider.py` (full — the module this phase's new provider mirrors), `backend/asos_provider.py` (full), `backend/weather_provider.py` (full, including `_fetch_grid`'s current Open-Meteo-only branch), `backend/dlr_pipeline.py::_token()`, `units_def.KNOWN_PROVIDER_NAMES`/`PIPELINE_SCHEMA`/`validate_pipeline_row`, `config.CITY_COORDINATES`, and both existing provider test files. Confirmed independently that `"nsrdb"` is already in `KNOWN_PROVIDER_NAMES` (so no schema change is needed), that `requests` 2.32.5 and `pandas` 2.3.3 are installed, that `.env` is gitignored and untracked, and that `pytest.ini` sets `pythonpath = backend` — so the plans' verify commands are grounded in this repository rather than assumed.
2. **Corrected an assumption `08-CONTEXT.md` carried into research.** The context document's "Claude's Discretion" note described `dlr_pipeline._token()` as raising `ValueError`; the research agent read the source and found it raises `EnvironmentError`. Rather than inheriting either reading, the plan settles the question with a stated reason: the new credential helpers raise `EnvironmentError` while response-validation failures raise `ValueError`, because Phase 10's fallback wiring (WX-06) must be able to fall back on a failing provider and refuse to fall back on a misconfigured machine. A test pins the two as distinct types so a future "simplification" cannot collapse them.
3. **Resolved the one Open Question research left genuinely open by design rather than by guess.** NSRDB requires a contact email on every request, which no existing provider in this codebase needs. The plan reads it from a second environment variable (`NREL_API_EMAIL`) with the same env-var-or-raise helper as the key, rather than hardcoding an address — a course submission should not carry a personal address, and a revoked registration should fail loudly rather than keep sending a stale value.
4. **Found one security consequence neither source document flagged.** NSRDB's live-captured JSON error body carries an `inputs` object echoing every request parameter back — including `api_key`. The obvious diagnostic gesture, quoting the error body into an exception message, is therefore also the one that writes the credential into a traceback or a log. Plan 08-02 adds a redaction helper, extracts only the `errors` array, and explicitly forbids `raise_for_status()` (whose exception carries the full request URL, and the key is a query parameter in it) — each pinned by a sentinel-value test and a source gate.
5. Designed around the two assumptions research could not verify without a real key (the exact `GHI Units` spelling; the exact out-of-range-year rejection wording) so that either outcome is safe: the units check is permissive on casing and spacing and skipped when the field is absent, with `PIPELINE_SCHEMA`'s range gate as an independent backstop, and error detection matches on status plus the generic errors-array shape with no test asserting any NREL sentence. Both are closed with recorded observed values by Plan 08-03's live checkpoint rather than silently resolved at plan time.
6. Treated the phase's headline silent-failure risk as the tracer's job: NSRDB's `wkt` parameter is longitude-first while `config.CITY_COORDINATES` and `_fetch_grid` are both latitude-first, and reversing it yields syntactically valid WKT for a different point on Earth — so nothing raises, the range check passes, and the row is still tagged with the caller's city. The swap is isolated in one named function, pinned by a literal-string test for Austin and a sign test across all four cities, and re-checked against the endpoint's own echoed coordinates in the live checkpoint.
7. Honoured `08-CONTEXT.md` D-01 (the NREL key is not yet registered) structurally rather than by assumption: Plans 08-01 and 08-02 are fully executable with no credential because every automated test mocks the HTTP call, and the two credential-dependent steps are `blocking-human` checkpoints in Plan 08-03 with an explicit "blocked" answer path, so an unissued key becomes a recorded blocker rather than an invisible one.
8. Verified the plans against GSD's own machine gates rather than by inspection: frontmatter and plan-structure validation on all three files, decision coverage (all five `08-CONTEXT.md` decisions and WX-03 cited across the set), and live shell-execution of representative verify-command forms (the `env -u` credential probe, the comment-filtered grep gates, the `git check-ignore`/`git ls-files` secret gates, and a multi-line `python -c` assertion) to confirm each actually runs in this environment with the stated failing direction.

Independently confirmed this session, and worth flagging because it contradicts what every other document in this repository currently says: **NREL's developer portal has been institutionally renamed to the National Laboratory of the Rockies.** `developer.nrel.gov` no longer resolves; the live host is `developer.nlr.gov`. This was confirmed twice over — the research agent probed the live endpoint directly, and the orchestrator verified the rename separately by web search against `energy.gov`, Wikipedia and `nlr.gov`. `data_provider.md` and `REQUIREMENTS.md` still cite the dead hostname; the correction is recorded in the ROADMAP and carried to Phase 10, which owns those documents.

No engineering code was written this session — this was planning only. The plans have not been executed, and both human checkpoints in Plan 08-03 are still open.

All 15 tests in the file passed immediately with zero production-code changes, since Task 1's tracer was already written to full production quality per its own instruction ("this is production code, not a prototype") — credential lookups already ran before any request was built, and the parse guard already rejected non-CSV bodies before `pd.read_csv` ever saw them. Per this project's TDD fail-fast convention, each assertion was individually re-checked against the underlying guard it claims to pin (not accepted as a vacuous pass) before committing as a single green `test(...)` commit.
4. Verified via the plan's own automated checks, not by inspection: `backend/tests/nsrdb_provider_test.py` (15/15), the `-k nsrdb` selection in `weather_provider_test.py` (4/4 in Task 1; 4/4 stable in Task 2), the `_wkt_point`/`NSRDB_ATTRIBUTES`/`NSRDB_INTERVAL_MINUTES`/`NSRDB_BASE`/`KNOWN_PROVIDER_NAMES` request-contract probe printed `nsrdb request contract OK`, the credential-less import probe printed `import without credentials OK`, the four-city `wkt`-order probe printed `four-city wkt order OK`, the credential fail-loud probe (missing key, email set, no live network reached) printed `credential fail-loud OK`, the `-k "dispatch or mock_grid or open_meteo"` Phase 6/7 regression group (14/14, unchanged), `git diff` confirming `weather_provider.py` edits confined to the import block/`_fetch_grid`/two docstrings, `git status --porcelain` confirming no change to `units_def.py` or `config.py`, and the full suite (270 passed, 0 failed).

Work happens on an isolated per-agent worktree branch, one `test(...)` (RED) commit and one `feat(...)` (GREEN) commit for Task 1, and one `test(...)` commit for Task 2 (no separate GREEN commit was needed — see item 3 above), following this project's established TDD split convention.

### Session: 2026-09-13, conducted by Aaron Phan — Phase 9 (TCEQ CAMS Integration) planning

Claude Code was used, via the GSD (`/gsd-plan-phase 9`) toolset running as a planner agent, to turn `09-CONTEXT.md`, `09-RESEARCH.md` and `09-PATTERNS.md` into three executable plan files for WX-01 (TCEQ CAMS as the primary wind/temp/RH station source, via HTML report scraping of `daily_summary.pl`).

1. Read the actual source before planning against it rather than trusting the upstream research documents: `backend/asos_provider.py` (full — the primary analog), `backend/weather_provider.py` (full, including `_fetch_station`'s current Synoptic-only branch and `attach_provenance`'s null-where-the-source-is-null invariant), `nsrdb_provider.py`'s per-period loop and `_range_check`, `units_def.py`'s unit registry and `PIPELINE_SCHEMA` ranges, `config.ASOS_STATIONS`, `requirements.txt`, `pytest.ini`, and both existing provider test files. Independently confirmed that `"tceq"` is already in `KNOWN_PROVIDER_NAMES` (so no schema change is needed), that `"mph"` and `"degF"` are already registered units, and that the pre-phase suite is at 296 passed — so the plans' verify commands and baselines are grounded in this repository rather than assumed.
2. **Corrected a factual error `09-RESEARCH.md` carried.** That document's Pitfall 8 states `requirements.txt` already contains an unpinned `bs4 4.14.3` line needing a `==` typo fix. It does not — the file declares only `pandas`, `numpy`, `scipy` and `pytest`. The `bs4` pin is therefore a genuinely **new** dependency, not a formatting repair, which makes the `[SUS]` legitimacy verdict in the same document's audit load-bearing: Plan 09-01 Task 1 is a blocking human checkpoint at pypi.org before the pin is written or the first `import bs4` lands. The research's optional `lxml` suggestion was declined outright so its own `[SUS]` flag never becomes live. Also noted, but deliberately not fixed: `requests` is likewise undeclared in `requirements.txt` despite three shipped provider modules importing it — recorded for the backlog rather than folded into a WX-01 phase.
3. **Reversed the research's own recommendation on one open design question, with a stated reason.** `09-RESEARCH.md` Open Question 1 recommends taking `stids[0]` when `fetch_weather` is called with multiple station IDs. That silently discards every other requested site while returning a complete, valid, provenance-tagged frame, with no signal to the caller. Because `daily_summary.pl` serves one site per request and the returned frame carries no station discriminator column, concatenating instead would let `merge_observations`' `(city, datetime_utc)` key stitch one row from two stations. Plan 09-02 raises, naming every supplied ID — consistent with this project's standing commitment that an unreliable input must never pass quietly.
4. **Answered the research's other open question rather than leaving it live.** No rate limit is documented for `daily_summary.pl` and none was confirmed during research, but D-09's per-calendar-day decomposition means a long backfill becomes hundreds of rapid sequential requests against a free, unauthenticated CGI script run by a state environmental agency. Plan 09-02 adds a self-imposed 31-day cap that raises before any request and names Open-Meteo (WX-04) as this project's bulk-historical source. The restraint is the project's own, not a limit the server enforces; it is also recorded as an explicit prohibition rather than only as code.
5. Treated the phase's two silent-corruption risks as the tracer's job, because neither produces an exception: the CGI ignores date parameters unless `select_date=user` is also sent, answering with *today's* report at HTTP 200 for any requested date; and TCEQ publishes in Local Standard Time year-round, so a DST-aware timezone conversion is wrong by exactly one hour for roughly eight months a year. Both are pinned by tests that a wrong implementation cannot pass — a recorder asserting the captured per-day date triples are *distinct*, and a July/January pair asserting hour `0000` converts identically at both dates.
6. Structurally closed research assumption A1 (that the report's per-row cell slice holds for all four sites, when only CAMS 83's table was diffed cell-by-cell) by deriving the hourly-cell slice from the header row's own hour labels rather than from a fixed 24-column or fixed-offset assumption — which also makes a partial same-day report align correctly instead of silently shifting. A3 (the four sites' fixed offsets) is closed by Plan 09-03's live checkpoint, not resolved at plan time.
7. Ran the phase's spec-less probe fallback in-prompt, since no `*-SPEC.md` exists: all three edge-coverage probe rows for WX-01 resolved to explicit acceptance criteria in `must_haves` (day-boundary adjacency and label-collision separation; four distinct empty shapes; sorted, de-duplicated, reproducible ordering), and a two-stage adversarial prohibition recall kept three values-level items — unthrottled consumption of a public government endpoint, never widening `PIPELINE_SCHEMA` ranges to make a TCEQ value fit, and never disguising the pipeline as a human browser. Nothing was silently dropped.
8. Verified the plans against GSD's own machine gates rather than by inspection: frontmatter and plan-structure validation on all three files (valid, zero errors, zero warnings), decision coverage (all nine `09-CONTEXT.md` decisions and WX-01 cited across the set, each in a gate-recognised location), the API-coverage and assumption-delta detectors, and live shell execution of the baseline suite and representative verify-command forms to confirm each actually runs in this environment with the stated failing direction. A capability-coverage matrix (`COVERAGE.md`) records which parts of the TCEQ report surface are integrated and, with a reason each, which nine are deliberately not — including TCEQ solar, which belongs to v2 requirement WX-09 and not to this milestone.

No engineering code was written this session — this was planning only. The plans have not been executed, and both blocking human checkpoints (the `bs4` legitimacy gate and the live four-site TCEQ confirmation) are still open.
