### Session: 2026-09-14 (continued), conducted by Aaron Phan — Phase 9 Plan 09-02 execution (multi-day decomposition, all four cities, fail-loud suite, GSD, isolated worktree)

Claude Code was used, via the GSD (`/gsd-execute-phase`) toolset running as an isolated-worktree executor agent (branch `worktree-agent-a3fd62ac3f0f1a7e5`), to execute `.planning/phases/09-tceq-cams-integration/09-02-PLAN.md` — expanding Plan 09-01's single-city single-day tracer to the full WX-01 surface: real multi-day window decomposition (D-09), all four CAMS cities including the two null-RH sites (D-04/D-05), the MST-vs-CST offset split, and the complete fail-loud suite (D-06/D-07, ROADMAP Success Criterion 4).

1. **Task 1** (TDD, `backend/tceq_provider.py`, `backend/tests/tceq_provider_test.py`): wrote 8 new tests (a recorder-based multi-day test asserting the captured per-day date triples are *distinct*, not merely equal in count — the exact silent-mis-dating failure 09-RESEARCH.md Pitfall 1 warns about; day-boundary adjacency; a deliberately-forced same-day-fetched-twice dedup test proving `keep="first"` semantics; reproducibility via `assert_frame_equal`; a zero-row window raise; a 40-day-over-cap raise asserting zero network calls; and ISO/bare-date window acceptance), confirmed RED (7 genuine failures — the multi-day/cap/bare-date tests failed because `_days_spanned`/`TCEQ_MAX_DAYS_PER_FETCH` did not yet exist or because `fetch_tceq` still carried Plan 09-01's single-UTC-day-only restriction), committed the RED state, then added `_to_utc_timestamp`/`_window_bounds` (ported from `nsrdb_provider`'s identical coercion) and `_days_spanned` (shifting each window bound by the site's fixed offset *before* taking the local calendar-date component, so a window is decomposed by the LOCAL day a report actually represents, not by the UTC day the caller happened to write), replaced `fetch_tceq`'s single-day restriction with a real per-local-day loop, and added `TCEQ_MAX_DAYS_PER_FETCH = 31` as a pre-request self-imposed cap. One correctness fix made during GREEN, not scoped as its own deviation rule since it was required for the reproducibility/dedup tests to hold deterministically: `combined.sort_values("datetime_utc")` was changed to pass `kind="stable"` explicitly, since pandas' default quicksort is not guaranteed stable and an unstable sort could reorder tied timestamps arbitrarily, silently breaking the documented "duplicates dropped keep='first' means the earlier-fetched day's value wins" contract. Also updated one Plan 09-01 tracer test whose premise no longer holds under correct local-day semantics: `_START`/`_END` (one *UTC* calendar day) actually spans *two local* calendar days for CAMS 83's -6 offset (local midnight of the requested day is 06:00Z, so the window's first six hours belong to the *previous* local day's report) — renamed and re-asserted the test to expect 2 requests instead of 1, with an explanatory comment, rather than silently deleting or skipping it. Full suite reached 316 passed (up from a 308-passed Plan 09-01 baseline).
2. **Task 2** (TDD, `backend/tceq_provider.py`, `backend/tests/tceq_provider_test.py`, `backend/tests/weather_provider_test.py`): wrote 12 new tests parameterized over all four `config.TCEQ_STATIONS` values (every city fetches successfully; Austin/Midland's absent `Relative Humidity` row nulls the column without raising; El Paso's -7 offset converts one hour later in UTC than the -6 Central sites for the identical local hour and date; an unknown CAMS ID raises before any request; `humidity_source` stays null on a null-RH `tceq` row while `wind_source`/`temp_source` read `"tceq"`, proving D-05 without touching `attach_provenance`). All 12 passed immediately with zero production-code changes beyond a documentation comment, since the RH-null handling, the offset table, and the unknown-ID raise were all already implemented as part of Task 1's rewrite (which already needed to handle every city's per-day construction correctly) — per this project's TDD fail-fast convention, each assertion was individually re-checked against the actual guard it claims to pin (e.g., confirming the offset-difference assertion would fail if El Paso's entry were accidentally set to -6) before accepting the pass as genuine, rather than accepted on faith. Added the per-site CST/MST evidence comment to `TCEQ_FIXED_UTC_OFFSET_HOURS` the task's action text calls for. Full suite reached 328 passed.
3. **Task 3** (TDD, `backend/tceq_provider.py`, `backend/tests/tceq_provider_test.py`, `backend/weather_provider.py`, `backend/tests/weather_provider_test.py`): wrote 10 new tests (the generic site-selection-form response raises; a well-formed report whose `<h1>` names a *different* CAMS ID than requested raises — already correctly implemented by Plan 09-01's `_validate_response`, which checks the href against the *requested* ID, not any ID; each of the three required rows raising when absent, pinned in the same parametrized group against RH-only-absence *not* raising, so the two dispositions can't silently collapse into one; placeholder (`FEW`/`NA`) cells nulling a value without dropping its row; a truncated row raising; and the multi-CAMS-ID guard). One genuine production-code change: `_fetch_station`'s `provider == "tceq"` branch previously took `stids[0]` silently on a multi-ID request (Plan 09-01's placeholder, matching 09-RESEARCH.md Open Question 1's original — since-reversed — recommendation); added a raise naming every supplied CAMS ID before calling `fetch_tceq` at all, since `daily_summary.pl` serves one site per request and the returned frame carries no station discriminator column, so silently taking the first ID would return a complete, valid, provenance-tagged frame for one city while discarding every other requested site with no signal to the caller. Confirmed RED for the two `weather_provider.py` tests genuinely requiring this guard (`test_tceq_rejects_multiple_cams_ids` failed with "DID NOT RAISE"); the `tceq_provider.py`-only tests in this task passed immediately against Plan 09-01/Task-1's already-correct `_validate_response`/`_extract_row_values`, confirmed genuine per the same re-check convention as Task 2. Full suite reached 338 passed, 0 failed.

Verified `docs/data-science/PRD.md` and `docs/data-science/decisions/data_provider.md` remained byte-identical (`git diff --name-only` empty for both) — continuing the four-phase control Phases 6, 7, and 8 each upheld; ratifying the provider decision is WX-08/Phase 10's job.
2. **Task 2** (`checkpoint:human-verify`, `gate="blocking-human"`, `backend/tceq_provider.py`, `backend/tests/tceq_provider_test.py`): ran every automation step the checkpoint's `<how-to-verify>` names — a real, unmocked `fetch_tceq` per CAMS site, a live fetch of all four `daily_summary.pl` pages to confirm the CST/MST sentence, a grep for the four exact row labels, and the invalid-CAMS-ID raise check — since this project's convention is that Claude performs all automation and the human only reviews/confirms, never runs a CLI command themselves. The first run against all four sites raised `"no site-specific report found"` for every site, which is exactly the finding the live check exists to surface: investigating the raw HTML revealed the live report page now renders **two** `<h1>` elements (a generic "Texas Commission on Environmental Quality" banner first, the site-specific title carrying the CAMS link second), while `_validate_response` checked only `soup.find("h1")` — the first one — so it rejected every genuine live report as if it were the invalid-site page. Fixed by searching every `<h1>` for the site link (confirmed the invalid-site page still carries no CAMS link in either `<h1>` and no `border='1'` table, so the fix does not weaken that check). Re-running then produced `"selected zero rows"` for all four sites; further investigation found the live report's header row is rendered entirely with `<th>` cells, while `_hour_labels` searched only `<td>` cells, silently returning zero hour labels for every fetched day (not a crash or a raise — a fetch that quietly contributed 0 rows). Fixed by searching both `<th>` and `<td>` in the header row (data rows were confirmed still `<td>`-only on the live page, so `_extract_row_values` needed no change). Added two regression tests (`test_fetch_tceq_finds_the_site_link_in_a_non_first_h1`, `test_fetch_tceq_extracts_hour_labels_from_th_header_cells`), each confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code before being accepted as green — both bugs were invisible to every existing mocked test because both Plan 09-01/09-02 fixtures matched the report shape research recorded rather than the shape the live page renders today. Both fixes are classified as Rule 1 (auto-fix bugs): they correct code that did not work as intended against the real endpoint, discovered only because this task's whole purpose is contact with reality. After both fixes, the live re-run returned real, plausible data for all four sites:

   ```
   date fetched: 2026-09-13
   AUSTIN           CAMS 38    rows= 24 first=2026-09-13 00:00:00+00:00 temp=[33.5] wind=[2.101088] dir=[145.0] RH=null (expected at 38/1095)
   CORPUS_CHRISTI   CAMS 83    rows= 24 first=2026-09-13 00:00:00+00:00 temp=[30.333333333333332] wind=[5.990336] dir=[143.0] RH=present
   MIDLAND          CAMS 1095  rows= 24 first=2026-09-13 00:00:00+00:00 temp=[36.72222222222222] wind=[7.37616] dir=[134.0] RH=null (expected at 38/1095)
   EL_PASO          CAMS 41    rows= 24 first=2026-09-13 00:00:00+00:00 temp=[35.333333333333336] wind=[1.609344] dir=[252.0] RH=present
   ```

   All four `air_temp` values are plausible September degrees-Celsius for their city (not a skipped-conversion Fahrenheit number), all four `wind_speed` values are plausible m/s (not mph), all four `wind_direction` values fall in 0-360, and RH is present at 83/41 and null at 38/1095 exactly as the CAMS Site Selection table predicts. The four timezone sentences were independently re-fetched via `curl` (not through `tceq_provider`, to avoid confirming the module against itself): CAMS 38, 83, and 1095 each render "All times shown are in CST"; CAMS 41 renders "All times shown are in MST" — matching `TCEQ_FIXED_UTC_OFFSET_HOURS` exactly. All four target row labels (`Wind Speed`, `Wind Direction`, `Outdoor Temperature`, `Relative Humidity`) were confirmed present verbatim in the live CAMS 83 page. The invalid-CAMS-ID check was re-run against the fixed code and raised `ValueError: tceq_provider: no site-specific report found for CAMS '999999'...` as expected — a clean raise, not a crash. Because the checkpoint's `gate="blocking-human"` explicitly excludes it from auto-approval regardless of automation mode, this session did not self-approve the checkpoint: all automation and evidence-gathering above was completed so the human's review is a confirmation of already-gathered evidence, not a manual re-run of these steps themselves.
3. Also recording, per this plan's own instruction, the two places this phase's execution diverged from its own research, closing the phase's disclosure obligation now that Plan 09-03 is the last plan in Phase 9: **09-RESEARCH.md Pitfall 8** asserted `requirements.txt` already contained an unpinned `bs4 4.14.3` line needing only an `==` fix; it did not (confirmed by reading the file directly during Plan 09-01) — the `beautifulsoup4==4.14.3` pin was a genuinely new dependency declaration, gated behind a blocking human legitimacy checkpoint before the first `import bs4` landed. **09-RESEARCH.md Open Question 1** recommended taking `stids[0]` when `fetch_weather` is called with more than one CAMS ID; this phase deliberately reversed that into a raise (Plan 09-02 Task 3), since `daily_summary.pl` serves one site per request and the returned frame carries no station discriminator column, so silently taking the first ID would discard every other requested site with no signal to the caller.

All work through Task 1 and Task 2's automation happened on the executor's assigned isolated worktree branch (`worktree-agent-a44dcd67392cf1afe`): one `test(...)` commit for the no-credential gate, one `docs(...)` commit for the C4 spec, and one `fix(...)` commit for the two live-check bug fixes plus their regression tests. No deviation in this session required a Rule 4 (architectural) escalation — both production fixes were narrow, load-bearing corrections to parsing logic that mocked tests could not have exercised. Per this worktree-executor convention, `.planning/STATE.md` and `.planning/ROADMAP.md` were deliberately left untouched — the orchestrator owns those writes after this worktree agent completes. Task 2's checkpoint (`gate="blocking-human"`) remains open pending the human's review of the evidence recorded above; `09-03-SUMMARY.md` records this session's state as `status: halted` per the summary template's convention for an intentional, designed stop, not a finished plan.

### Session: 2026-09-14 (continued), conducted by Aaron Phan — post-v1.1 development-tracks documentation

Claude Code was used, in a direct (non-GSD-phase) conversation, to investigate and document what DS-subsystem work genuinely remains after the v1.1 milestone, for dividing across developers. This was investigative/documentation work, not code: Claude read `dlr_pipeline.py`, `corridor_discretization.py`, `ampacity_forecast.py`, `anomaly_detector.py`, `thermal_model.py`, and their test files directly (rather than trusting `PRD.md`'s existing claims) to establish, with evidence, that (1) `run_pipeline` never calls `rate_corridor`, `AnomalyDetector`, or `forecast_ampacity` — they are three code paths connected only by their own test suites, not one pipeline; (2) `ampacity_forecast.py`/`anomaly_detector.py` contain no ML model — only naive/EWM statistical methods and a `mean ± k·std` threshold — and are tested exclusively against synthetic fixtures, never real data; and (3) `backend/config.py::CORRIDOR_ENDPOINTS` uses synthetic city-centroid corridor geometry, which the System Design Report itself flags as a placeholder pending real transmission-line geometry.

Based on these findings, Claude authored `docs/data-science/DEVELOPMENT-TRACKS.md`, outlining three independent post-v1.1 workstreams (pipeline integration & real-data verification; ML model development; real corridor geometry), and corrected `docs/data-science/PRD.md` §8 ("Future Scope"), which had gone stale since v1.0 shipped — it previously claimed C8/C9/C10 had "no corresponding code today," which stopped being true at v1.0 but was never updated. §8 now states the actual current-code status and points to the new tracks document instead of restating it.

No production code was changed in this session.

### Session: 2026-09-14 (continued), conducted by Aaron Phan — GSD workstream scoping for three post-v1.1 tracks

Claude Code was used, via GSD (`/gsd-new-milestone`, subagent `gsd-roadmapper`), to migrate the existing v1.1 planning state into a GSD workstream and scope three independent parallel workstreams — `pipeline-integration` (v1.2), `ml-modeling` (v1.3), and `corridor-geometry` (v1.4) — corresponding to the three tracks in `docs/data-science/DEVELOPMENT-TRACKS.md`. For each, Claude gathered scope through conversational questioning (goal confirmation, category-level requirement scoping via multi-select options, full requirements-list confirmation), then wrote `REQUIREMENTS.md` and spawned a `gsd-roadmapper` subagent per workstream to derive a phased roadmap grounded in the actual codebase (reading `dlr_pipeline.py`, `corridor_discretization.py`, `ampacity_forecast.py`, `anomaly_detector.py`, `historical_archive.py`, `config.py`, and course documents directly, not paraphrasing from memory) — each roadmap was presented for explicit human approval before being committed.

Two decisions of note: (1) the `corridor-geometry` roadmapper subagent identified that Phase 1 (obtaining real transmission-line geometry) is an external coordination dependency on the Power Systems sub-team that the team does not yet have, and deliberately scoped Phase 1 to only what the DS side can unilaterally complete (a data contract + formal request) rather than assuming external data arrives on schedule, explicitly marking Phase 2 as blocked; (2) after the user identified a missing "data cleaning" requirement, Claude added CLEAN-01/02/03 to `pipeline-integration`'s `REQUIREMENTS.md` and re-spawned the roadmapper in revision mode, which — grounded in reading `historical_archive.py`'s actual `read_history`/`append_observation` functions — determined that a new Data Cleaning phase must be inserted *before* the existing Real-Data Validation phase (renumbering it), since validation's forecast/anomaly calibration findings would otherwise measure archive defects (stuck sensors, gaps, duplicate rows) rather than real signal.

No production code was changed in this session — all work was planning-document creation (`.planning/workstreams/*/REQUIREMENTS.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `STATE.md`) via git-committed GSD workflow steps.

### Session: 2026-09-14 (continued), conducted by Aaron Phan — Phase 10 Plan 10-01 execution (TCEQ-to-Synoptic fallback tracer + no-fallback taxonomy, GSD, isolated worktree)

Claude Code was used, via the GSD (`/gsd-execute-phase`) toolset running as an isolated-worktree executor agent (branch `worktree-agent-a564db80c21d958c1`), to execute `.planning/workstreams/multi-provider-weather/phases/10-fallback-wiring-documentation-ratification/10-01-PLAN.md` — wiring Synoptic as the automatic fallback behind `weather_provider.fetch_weather` for one failure scenario end to end (WX-06's tracer) and then proving the fallback stays inert for every caller-shaped and configuration error that isn't a primary-provider data failure.

1. **Task 1** (tracer, TDD, `backend/config.py`, `backend/weather_provider.py`, `backend/tests/weather_provider_test.py`): added `config.SYNOPTIC_FALLBACK_STATIONS` (the four `CITY_COORDINATES` keys mapped to their primary-airport Synoptic ICAO IDs, `KAUS`/`KCRP`/`KMAF`/`KELP`) and `weather_provider`'s fallback registry (`FALLBACK_PROVIDER`, `FALLBACK_ELIGIBLE_PRIMARIES = {"tceq", "nsrdb"}`, `STATION_PATH_PROVIDERS`, `GRID_PATH_PROVIDERS`) plus `_fallback_city_for_request`, `_fallback_station_ids`, and `_attempt_fallback`. Rewrote `fetch_weather` to accept a keyword-only `fallback: bool = True`: an eligibility flag computed immediately after `_validate_location_shape` (before any provider is called) gates whether the dispatch runs inside a `try` at all, so a caller-shaped error structurally cannot reach the fallback rather than merely being tested not to. `EnvironmentError` is re-raised via its own `except` clause before the `ValueError` handler; a `ValueError` or an empty/`None` result routes through `_attempt_fallback`, which re-raises the original error unchanged when no fallback station resolves, or retries via `_fetch_station(..., FALLBACK_PROVIDER)` — inheriting the ASOS supplement and provenance tagging rather than reimplementing them — and wraps a fallback-side failure in a `ValueError` naming both providers and the city, chained `from` the original primary error. Wrote 5 new tests under a `Plan 10-01 Task 1` banner (all containing the substring `fallback` per the plan's own `-k "fallback"` verify command), confirmed they exercise the real fallback path (a monkeypatched `dlr_pipeline.fetch_timeseries` return value with no `lat` column, so `_supplement_with_asos` takes its own no-op skip branch and no test in this phase attempts a live ASOS or Synoptic network call). Full suite reached 346 passed (up from a 338-passed Plan 09-03 baseline) at this task's boundary.
2. **Task 2** (TDD, `backend/tests/weather_provider_test.py`): added a `Plan 10-01 Task 2` banner with a shared counting spy on `dlr_pipeline.fetch_timeseries` (the network entry point every fallback attempt ultimately goes through) and 12 new tests covering every fail-loud class WX-06 must never swallow: unknown provider names on both paths, `nsrdb`/`open_meteo` requested through a station list, `tceq` requested through a coordinate tuple, all five invalid `location` shapes, the multi-CAMS-ID guard, non-finite `nsrdb` coordinates, a failing `synoptic` call (which has nowhere to fall back to), a failing `open_meteo` call (deliberately not fallback-eligible), an `EnvironmentError` from `nsrdb` propagating unmasked (a missing `NREL_API_KEY` means the operator hasn't finished setup, not that the primary is failing — T-10-06), a `caplog`-based assertion that a successful fallback logs a WARNING naming both providers (T-10-02), and a credential-leak guard asserting a both-sides-failed error message carries neither a monkeypatched `SYNOPTIC_TOKEN` value nor the substring `token` (T-10-03). Verified via `git diff --diff-filter=D` (no unexpected deletions) and a direct read of `git diff -- backend/tests/weather_provider_test.py` (320 lines, insertions only, confirming no pre-existing test body was touched) that all six pre-existing tests the plan names as must-still-pass-unmodified (`test_tceq_rejects_multiple_cams_ids`, `test_nsrdb_is_not_reachable_from_the_station_path`, `test_grid_path_still_rejects_unknown_provider_names`, `test_nsrdb_grid_fetch_raises_on_unresolvable_coordinates`, `test_open_meteo_grid_fetch_raises_on_unresolvable_coordinates`, `test_asos_failure_propagates_out_of_fetch_weather`) still pass byte-identical to their pre-Phase-10 form. Full suite reached 363 passed, 0 failed.
3. This is a `type: tdd` plan; both tasks' RED/GREEN sequencing is documented per-commit rather than as separate `test(...)`/`feat(...)` commits, since the fallback registry, the helper functions, and the eligibility-gate rewrite were implemented together as one coherent unit before any Plan 10-01 test was written or run — the plan's own `<action>` text specifies the full production-code shape up front rather than incrementally, and every acceptance criterion and `<verify>` command was independently re-run against the final state (registry equality checks, signature/resolution probes, `-k "fallback"` at 21/21, the full file at 101/101, and the whole repository at 363/363) before either commit, so no criterion was accepted on faith.

### Session: 2026-09-14 (continued), conducted by Aaron Phan — Phase 10 Plan 10-02 execution (grid-path fallback, four-city coverage, exhaustion, schema/merge integration, GSD, isolated worktree)

Claude Code was used, via the GSD (`/gsd-execute-phase`) toolset running as an isolated-worktree executor agent (branch `worktree-agent-a5fc269a3632e4d2f`), to execute `.planning/workstreams/multi-provider-weather/phases/10-fallback-wiring-documentation-ratification/10-02-PLAN.md` — expanding Plan 10-01's one-path tracer to the full WX-06 fallback surface: the NSRDB grid path, all four cities against both fallback-eligible primaries, the both-providers-exhausted case, and schema/merge integration for a fallback-sourced row.

1. **Task 1** (TDD, `backend/weather_provider.py`, `backend/tests/weather_provider_test.py`): added `_as_grid_shaped(frame, lat, lon)` -- the single seam reconciling a station-shaped Synoptic fallback frame with the grid path's `city`-column contract, returning the frame unchanged whenever `city` is already present as a column or an index level (the case when `_supplement_with_asos` has already re-keyed it), to avoid a duplicate-name collision inside `merge_observations._prepare_merge_side`'s `reset_index()` call. Extended `_attempt_fallback`'s grid branch to shape the fallback result via `_as_grid_shaped` and apply `_fetch_grid`'s own null-city guard, raising (chained from the primary error) rather than emitting an unattributable row. Documented in-line the decision not to narrow the fallback frame to solar alone: NSRDB is solar-only because NREL's own documentation forbids using its 2m reanalysis wind for a wind-power calculation, but Synoptic's wind is a real 10m station measurement, so the fallback frame is allowed to carry its full measured variable set -- `attach_provenance` still attributes every field honestly to `synoptic`, and `merge_observations` gives a station-side primary precedence anyway. Added 8 tests confirming city attribution, solar-source honesty (no fabricated 1000 W/m² value, `solar_source` null exactly where `solar_radiation` is null), and the ASOS-supplemented shape surviving without a duplicate-city error.
2. **Task 2** (TDD, `backend/weather_provider.py`, `backend/tests/weather_provider_test.py`): while implementing this task's own required behavior ("an empty fallback frame raises rather than being returned"), found and fixed a genuine Rule 1 bug in `_attempt_fallback` -- a `None`/empty Synoptic fallback result was silently returned as-is instead of raising, meaning a truly exhausted fallback could reach a caller looking like a normal (if data-sparse) result rather than a clear failure. Fixed by raising the same both-providers-failed `ValueError` (chained from the primary error) whenever the fallback call succeeds but returns nothing usable. Added 16 tests: 8 parametrized cases (4 cities x {tceq, nsrdb}) each asserting `dlr_pipeline.fetch_timeseries` received the exact per-city Synoptic station ID (not merely that a frame came back, which could mask a constant), an explicit `_fallback_station_ids` eight-combination coverage test, an `nsrdb`+`synoptic` exhaustion test naming both providers with `__cause__` chained to the original `nsrdb` error, the new empty-fallback-raises behavior for both legs, and `fallback=False` suppressing the fallback on both legs with a zero-call counter.
3. **Task 3** (TDD, `backend/tests/weather_provider_test.py`, this file): added tests proving a fallback-sourced row is a first-class pipeline row -- every row of a fallback-sourced NSRDB-substitute frame passes `units_def.validate_pipeline_row`, every non-null `*_source` value is in `units_def.KNOWN_PROVIDER_NAMES`, and (mirroring the Plan 07-03 three-provider proof's structure) a real live TCEQ station frame (Corpus Christi, CAMS 83) merged with a fallback-sourced grid frame (Corpus Christi, a failing `nsrdb` request substituted by Synoptic station KCRP) attributes `wind_source`/`temp_source` to `tceq`, the fallback-supplied `solar_radiation` (TCEQ has no solar sensor at this site) to `synoptic`, and no `*_source` cell anywhere to `nsrdb` -- the primary that was asked for and never actually supplied anything (ROADMAP SC2, T-10-10). Added a guard asserting `PIPELINE_SCHEMA_VERSION == 3` and the exact `PIPELINE_SCHEMA` key set is unchanged from the Phase 6 baseline, confirmed `backend/units_def.py` carries a zero-line diff for this phase (`git diff --name-only` empty), and confirmed the whole repository suite reached 388 passed, 0 failed (up from Plan 10-01's 363-passed baseline).

Work happened on the executor's assigned isolated worktree branch (`worktree-agent-a5fc269a3632e4d2f`): one `feat(...)` commit for Task 1, one `test(...)` commit for Task 2 (including the empty-fallback-frame bugfix), and one `test(...)` commit for Task 3 (schema/merge proof + this ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS.md entry), followed by a `docs(...)` commit for `10-02-SUMMARY.md`. No deviation in this session required a Rule 4 (architectural) escalation -- both fixes (the empty-fallback raise in Task 2, and the city-as-index-level guard fix discovered while writing Task 1's ASOS-shape test) were narrow Rule 1 corrections to code that did not yet behave as this phase's own required behavior specified. Read `01-CONTEXT.md` (the user's locked decisions D-01 through D-07 from `/gsd-discuss-phase`), `REQUIREMENTS.md`, `STATE.md`, and `ROADMAP.md` in full, then read the actual current implementation directly rather than relying on prior documentation: `backend/ampacity_forecast.py`, `backend/historical_archive.py`, `backend/thermal_model.py`, `backend/open_meteo_provider.py`, `backend/config.py`, `backend/units_def.py`, and `backend/corridor_discretization.py`'s corridor-rating/weakest-link-reduction logic, plus the prior `04-RESEARCH.md` (v1.0 Phase 4) that had originally deferred this exact ML upgrade pending real data volume.
2. Identified two load-bearing, previously-undocumented architectural findings from direct code reading: (a) `ampacity_forecast.py`'s private `_point_forecast`/`backtest_residuals` helpers currently take only a univariate `pd.Series`, so a new multivariate method needs a backward-compatible internal signature extension (not a change to the locked public `forecast_ampacity` signature) to see weather columns; and (b) `historical_archive.ARCHIVE_COLUMNS` already carries a `corridor` column and `read_history(corridor=...)` already filters by it, which directly answers the phase's open per-location "model selection without a signature change" question — the runtime frame already carries its own location identity.
3. Verified the project's actual installed environment directly (not from stale `STACK.md`/training-data assumptions): confirmed via the real `.venv` (`.venv/bin/python3`, Python 3.12.13) that `scikit-learn`/`statsmodels`/`xgboost` are absent, and via `.venv/bin/python3 -m pip index versions` against the live PyPI registry that `scikit-learn` 1.9.1 is current. Ran this project's package-legitimacy check tool (`gsd_run query package-legitimacy check --ecosystem pypi scikit-learn statsmodels xgboost`) this session — all three returned `[SUS]` (incomplete download/repo metadata, not a real red flag), reproducing `04-RESEARCH.md`'s own prior finding for the same packages. Per the user's own explicit CONTEXT.md decision (D-02), the human-approval checkpoint that verdict would otherwise require is pre-waived for this phase — the verification scan itself was still run and documented, per the user's own D-03 instruction that the scan is not skipped, only the extra approval gate on top of it.
4. Ran a small number of live web searches (Open-Meteo Archive API request limits, `scikit-learn` `TimeSeriesSplit`/model-persistence documentation, walk-forward-validation computational-cost tradeoffs) to ground the recommended architecture pattern (fit a model once offline per corridor, persist via `joblib`, and reuse the existing `backtest_residuals` walk-forward machinery against the *fixed* model at call time rather than retraining per row) — each finding is tagged `[CITED]`/`[ASSUMED]` in the output document by source, and the Open-Meteo rate-limit claim is explicitly flagged low-confidence (general web search, not a live probe against this project's own actual multi-year pull) pending a real smoke-test call during execution.
5. Wrote `.planning/workstreams/ml-modeling/phases/01-forecasting-model/01-RESEARCH.md`, including an Architectural Responsibility Map, a Package Legitimacy Audit (reproducing the live scan above), a recommended offline-training/online-inference architecture (with a system diagram), a "Don't Hand-Roll" table, Common Pitfalls (including an explicit warning that `dlr_amps` is a deterministic function of concurrent weather, so a model must never be fed weather from at-or-after the forecast target time or it would silently "cheat" by re-deriving physics rather than forecasting), an Assumptions Log (five items, e.g. the corridor-level training target's two-endpoint-city `min()` proxy for the true future bottleneck-point weather), a Validation Architecture section mapping FCST-01/02 to concrete `pytest` commands, and a Security Domain section flagging a genuinely new threat surface this phase introduces (untrusted `joblib`/pickle deserialization of a persisted model artifact).

This session's most consequential AI-generated content is the recommended architecture pattern in item 2 above — a not-yet-existing design (the internal-helper signature extension and the corridor-key-based per-location model selection), not a description of code that already exists. It is presented as a recommendation grounded in verbatim-quoted existing code, not as a settled implementation; the specific model class (regression vs. neural network per D-01) is explicitly left as an open, evidence-driven decision for the planning/execution phase, not decided by this research session, per the user's own explicit instruction that no default lean is permitted.

Independently re-confirmed the environment facts the plans depend on (`.venv` Python 3.12.13; `scikit-learn` absent; the existing `ampacity_forecast_test.py` suite green at 13 passed in 0.44s) rather than carrying them forward from the research document.
2. Produced three plans in three waves: `01-01` (a `tracer` task wiring one real end-to-end path — pinned dependency, an offline `backend/forecast_training.py` assembling Open-Meteo Archive backfill through `thermal_model.ieee738_ampacity` into a per-corridor series, a fitted estimator persisted per corridor, and `forecast_ampacity(history, method="ml")` returning the same four `PIPELINE_SCHEMA` columns — plus the invariant tests), `01-02` (D-01's two-candidate bake-off scored against each other and against naive/EWM on the identical series, both corridors), and `01-03` (FCST-02's written validation record and the D-07 correction of the stale `C9-ampacity-forecasting.md` spec).
3. Two architectural calls were made by the planner rather than carried from the research document, and both are consequential enough to name here. First, the feature-engineering implementation was placed in the *runtime* module (`ampacity_forecast.build_ml_features`) with the offline training script importing it, rather than the reverse — the research document's recommended structure implied the opposite dependency direction, which would have produced two feature-builder implementations free to drift apart at exactly the boundary where a mismatch is silent. Second, the planner found a breakage the research document did not flag: the existing `test_band_contains_point_forecast_and_types_are_native` is parametrized over `FORECAST_METHODS`, so adding a third method value causes it to demand a model artifact that is git-ignored and absent from a clean checkout; the plan makes fixing that test part of the same task that extends the tuple, via a monkeypatch-able loader seam and a fixture that fits a tiny real estimator in memory.
4. The plans deliberately do **not** insert a `checkpoint:human-verify` before the `scikit-learn` install, even though this project's standing protocol requires one for a `[SUS]` legitimacy verdict, because the user's own CONTEXT.md decision D-02 explicitly pre-waives that gate for this dependency class. This deviation is recorded in each plan's threat model (`T-01-SC`) so it reads as a user decision rather than an omitted control; the legitimacy scan itself (required by D-03) was run during the prior research session and is not skipped.
5. Where the phase had no `SPEC.md`, the planner ran the spec-less fallback protocol: the deterministic edge probe returned both of its items unresolved/unclassified, so both are carried into the plans as explicitly flagged assumptions rather than dropped — the training job's interruption/parallel-run behavior (resolved by design: not resumable, but artifact writes are atomic via temp-file-then-`os.replace`, so a killed or concurrent run cannot leave a torn model file for the runtime loader) and FCST-02's unspecified validation-record format (resolved by the planner enumerating its required contents). The planner also ran the two-stage prohibition recall, keeping a small number of must-NOT constraints specific to a line-rating system's core hazard — chiefly that a `ForecastResult` must never report `method="ml"` for a number that actually came from a degraded path, and that the confidence band must never be narrower than the real out-of-sample residuals support. `joblib`/pickle deserialization was recognized as canon security and deliberately left to the threat model rather than minted as a bespoke prohibition.
6. Every `<automated>` verification command written into the plans was syntax-checked this session (the embedded Python one-liners parsed with `ast.parse`; the shell gates dry-run against a dummy fixture, including a negative case confirming a missing token actually fails the gate) rather than assumed runnable. One planner-authored expectation is flagged as the planner's own judgment, not sourced from any project document: the bar the model is judged against in the validation record is "the chosen candidate's held-out MAE must be below both naive's and ewm's on the identical series," chosen because no project document contains an independently sourced accuracy target and inventing a specific amp threshold would have been a fabricated baseline.

This session's most consequential AI-generated content is the plan set itself — a set of instructions for future work, not an implementation. The estimator choice (regression vs. neural network) remains explicitly undecided and is to be settled by the measured bake-off in Plan `01-02`, per the user's D-01 instruction that no default lean is permitted; the plans are written so that both candidates' measured numbers must survive into the validation record, so that instruction is demonstrable from the evidence rather than merely asserted.

1. **Task 1** (`type="tracer"`): Installed `scikit-learn==1.9.1` into the project's `.venv` (symlinked into this worktree, since a fresh worktree checkout has no `.venv` of its own) and pinned it in `requirements.txt`, replacing the stale "Tier 2 modelling libraries... deliberately absent" comment block per CONTEXT.md D-02's pre-waiver of the human-legitimacy checkpoint that verdict would otherwise require (the scan itself already ran in `01-RESEARCH.md`). Added `/data/models/` to `.gitignore`. Added seven new `FORECAST_ML_*` tunables to `backend/config.py` (model dir/filename templates, lag hours, rolling window, training years, feature weather columns). Extended `backend/ampacity_forecast.py`: `FORECAST_METHODS` gained `"ml"`; added `ML_FEATURE_COLUMNS`, `build_ml_features` (lag + rolling-mean + weather + cyclical hour-of-day features, each lag/rolling value shifted so it never sees its own or a later row), `_extract_ml_frame` (parses `datetime_utc` into a real `DatetimeIndex`, raises rather than silently falling back to a positional index), `_corridor_of` (validates against `config.CORRIDOR_ENDPOINTS` before any path is built — the path-traversal guard for the model artifact), `_model_path_for`, `_MODEL_CACHE`/`_load_corridor_model` (lazy `joblib` import inside the function body, so `import ampacity_forecast` stays free of `sklearn`/`joblib`), and extended `_point_forecast`/`backtest_residuals` with optional `history`/`model` parameters that `"naive"`/`"ewm"` never use. Created `backend/forecast_training.py` (new file): `fetch_city_weather` (one Open-Meteo Archive API call per complete calendar year per city), `corridor_training_frame` (real IEEE-738 ampacity for both of a corridor's endpoint cities, reduced via the codebase's established weakest-link `min()` convention, every assembled row validated through `units_def.validate_pipeline_row` before it can reach the estimator), `build_training_matrix` (target = `dlr_amps` shifted forward by exactly one horizon's worth of rows; returns X indexed by feature timestamp and y indexed by target timestamp so the no-leakage relationship is directly inspectable, not just asserted in prose), `fit_corridor_model` (`HistGradientBoostingRegressor`, fixed `random_state`), `persist_corridor_model` (atomic temp-file-then-`os.replace`), and a `--corridor`/`--years` CLI. Extended `backend/tests/ampacity_forecast_test.py` with a `varying_weather` flag on `_synthetic_history` and a `fitted_ml_model` fixture (a genuinely fitted, in-memory `HistGradientBoostingRegressor`, no disk I/O or network) so the pre-existing `test_band_contains_point_forecast_and_types_are_native` (parametrized over `FORECAST_METHODS`) keeps passing once `"ml"` joins the tuple.
2. Ran the plan's full live verification chain rather than trusting the code in isolation: `.venv/bin/python3 backend/forecast_training.py --corridor AUSTIN_CORPUS_CHRISTI --years 1` against the real, live Open-Meteo Archive API (8760 hourly rows, 2025-01-01 through 2025-12-31, ~15s), confirmed the persisted `.joblib` and training-frame CSV both exist, then loaded that CSV and called `forecast_ampacity(frame, method="ml")` directly — confirming its `amps` differs from both the last observed value and the `method="ewm"` result on the identical frame, its band is non-degenerate (8748 out-of-sample residuals), and `forecast_columns()` still produces exactly the four `PIPELINE_SCHEMA` keys. Also grep-verified `backend/ampacity_forecast.py` contains no estimator-`.fit()` call and no null/forward/back-fill or interpolation call, and that `import ampacity_forecast` puts neither `sklearn` nor `joblib` into `sys.modules`. Ran the full `pytest` suite twice (before and after the test-file extension): 388 passed before, 389 passed after (the new `"ml"` parametrization case), 0 failed both times.
3. No deviation in this session required a Rule 4 (architectural) escalation. One environment gap was resolved directly rather than deferred: this fresh worktree checkout had no `.venv` of its own (each worktree is a separate working-tree clone; `.venv` is git-ignored, not part of the checkout), so a symlink to the shared checkout's already-provisioned `.venv` (`/Users/aphan/Senior_Design/.venv`, where `scikit-learn` was also installed this session) was created inside the worktree — a one-time, git-ignored local setup step, not a plan deviation.
4. **Task 2** (`type="auto" tdd="true"`): Added `backend/tests/forecast_training_test.py` (new file, 3 tests: `no_future_leakage` on `build_training_matrix`'s feature/target timestamps, `weakest_link_min_matches_convention` — constructing two endpoint cities whose relative ambient temperature alternates which one is the corridor's bottleneck, then checking the reduction's `dlr_amps`/`bottleneck_index`/weather-provenance together against ground truth computed via the same `thermal_model` functions the code under test calls, and `invalid_row_dropped_before_fit` — an out-of-range winning-city temperature that must be dropped, not silently degrade the row's recorded weather) and extended `backend/tests/ampacity_forecast_test.py` with 7 more tests (a mutation-based `build_ml_features` leakage check; `ml_method_sensitive_to_weather_naive_ewm_unaffected` asserting both directions in one test; two `corridor_model_selection` tests for mixed/unknown corridors; `missing_artifact_raises_and_never_falls_back`; and two null/missing-weather refusal-to-impute tests). All ten tests passed against Task 1's existing implementation on first run with no production-code changes required — one test's own initial scenario (an extreme temperature intended to produce an out-of-range row) instead produced a `NaN` ampacity that `validate_pipeline_row` treats as a not-required, therefore non-violating, absent value, so the test was corrected to use a milder out-of-range temperature that stays numerically finite; this was a test-authoring fix, not a production-code deviation. 1. Discovered the assigned worktree's branch was stale (`backend/forecast_training.py` and the `"ml"` method were both absent), older than Plan 01-01's merge commit `5819b36`. Verified the worktree had zero local commits and a clean working tree, then fast-forwarded (`git merge --ff-only main`) onto `main`'s tip — a non-destructive operation carrying no risk of discarding worktree-local work, since none existed yet.
2. **Task 1** (`type="auto" tdd="true"`): Added two new `config.py` tunables (`FORECAST_ML_BAKEOFF_SPLITS=5`, `FORECAST_ML_BAKEOFF_REPORT_NAME`). Wrote failing tests first (RED — `AttributeError: module 'forecast_training' has no attribute 'bakeoff_corridor'`, five failures) covering `bakeoff_corridor`'s shape/finiteness, `TimeSeriesSplit`'s no-fold-overlap guarantee, `baseline_scores`' exact numerical agreement with `ampacity_forecast.backtest_residuals` run directly, `corridor_report`'s four-candidate/window-field shape, and `select_winner`'s symmetric MAE-driven selection (both orderings) plus its refusal to pick a winner on a missing or non-finite measurement — committed as a `test(01-02)` commit. Then implemented (GREEN) `backend/forecast_training.py`: `CANDIDATE_ESTIMATORS` (an ordered, two-entry mapping of zero-argument factories — `HistGradientBoostingRegressor` for the regression arm, a `StandardScaler`+`MLPRegressor` pipeline for the neural-network arm, the scaler added specifically so the comparison measures architecture rather than a missing preprocessing step), `bakeoff_corridor` (pooled MAE/RMSE per candidate across `TimeSeriesSplit(n_splits=config.FORECAST_ML_BAKEOFF_SPLITS)` folds, a fresh unfitted estimator per fold, no hand-rolled slicing loop), `baseline_scores` (naive/ewm scored through the existing `backtest_residuals` — the apples-to-apples requirement, not a second error computation), `corridor_report` (merges both into a four-entry `candidates` mapping plus the assembled row count and window span), `select_winner` (reads only the two `CANDIDATE_ESTIMATORS` names' measured MAE, raises `ValueError` on a missing or non-finite entry, deterministic rmse-then-declaration-order tiebreak logged at INFO), and refactored `fit_corridor_model` to take an already-constructed, unfitted estimator so it serves both the tracer's original single fit and the bake-off winner's full-window refit. `main` gained a `--bakeoff` flag; without it, behavior is unchanged from Plan 01-01. All six targeted tests passed on the first implementation attempt; full suite went from 399 to 406 passed, 0 failed. Confirmed `backend/ampacity_forecast.py` stayed byte-identical (this task's own acceptance criterion) via `git diff --stat`.
3. **Task 2** (`type="auto" tdd="true"`): Parameterized `_synthetic_history` with a `corridor` argument (default unchanged) and added two new isolation tests to `backend/tests/ampacity_forecast_test.py`: one building two genuinely fitted `HistGradientBoostingRegressor`s from the identical feature frame but targets offset by a large, unmistakable constant (500,000 A), monkeypatching `_load_corridor_model` to dispatch on the corridor argument, and asserting both corridors' frames get their own model's prediction across three call orders (Austin-then-Midland, Midland-then-Austin, Austin-called-twice-consecutively) with no cross-corridor drift; a second test exercising the REAL (non-stubbed) `_load_corridor_model`/`_MODEL_CACHE` with only `joblib.load` monkeypatched, confirming the cache is genuinely keyed per corridor and a second call for an already-seen corridor never triggers a reload. Both tests passed immediately against the existing, unmodified `ampacity_forecast.py` — Plan 01-01 had already keyed `_MODEL_CACHE` correctly by corridor, so per the task's own "if Plan 01 already got this right, no edit is needed" instruction, that file was left untouched; this was confirmed, not assumed. Updated `forecast_training.py`'s plain (non-`--bakeoff`) corridor loop and `_run_bakeoff` so a failure assembling one corridor is reported by name and the run continues to the next corridor rather than aborting silently, and added a final `SUMMARY:` line per corridor (rows, window span, winner, persisted path) to `_run_bakeoff`'s stdout. Full suite: 409 passed, 0 failed.
4. Executed the plan's required real, full-window, two-corridor bake-off against the live Open-Meteo Archive API (`--bakeoff`, no `--corridor`, default `config.FORECAST_ML_TRAINING_YEARS=3`, run in the background given its expected multi-minute duration — completed in a little over 6 minutes): both corridors assembled 26,304 rows spanning 2023-01-01 through 2025-12-31 (the full intended 3-year window, not shortened), fit both candidates via the 5-fold bake-off, and persisted a winner (`MLPRegressor` won both corridors by MAE among the two ML candidates specifically — `HistGradientBoostingRegressor` 79.094/`MLPRegressor` 77.701 for Austin↔Corpus Christi, 90.127/89.593 for Midland↔El Paso). **The honest, load-bearing finding from this live run**: on this real held-out data, `naive` (67.173 MAE, Austin↔Corpus Christi; 79.755 MAE, Midland↔El Paso) beat BOTH ML candidates on both corridors — the winning `MLPRegressor` is roughly 10.5 A (15.7%) worse than naive on Austin↔Corpus Christi and roughly 9.8 A (12.3%) worse than naive on Midland↔El Paso. This is the opposite of Pitfall 4's "too good to be true" leakage signature (which would show near-zero or implausibly-better-than-naive error); it is instead a plausible, unflattering result for a highly autocorrelated hourly physical series at a 1-hour horizon, where the last observed value is already a strong predictor. This judgment — that the result shows no leakage signature but also does not demonstrate an accuracy improvement over naive — is recorded explicitly in `01-02-SUMMARY.md` rather than silently treated as a passing result. Independently re-ran the full `pytest` suite (409 passed) and the isolation-test subset after the live run to confirm nothing regressed; confirmed `git status --porcelain data/models` is empty (the live run's artifacts and report are git-ignored generated evidence, as designed).

### Session: 2026-09-29 through 2026-09-30, conducted by Aaron Phan — `ml-modeling` workstream Phase 3 Waves 2-5 execution (Plans 03-03 through 03-07: the D-04 pivot to the 24-hour realizable lead, the seven-arm held-out comparison, and the completed decision record, isolated worktrees)

Claude Code was used, via the GSD (`/gsd-execute-phase`) toolset running as isolated-worktree executor agents across five separate plan executions, to build and close out Phase 3's D-04 replan after Wave 1 (Plans 03-01/03-02, separately acknowledged above) found the originally-scoped 1-hour idealized-lead data source bit-identical to truth. This consolidated entry covers all five plans of Waves 2-5 in one disclosure, per this phase's own deliberate deferral of the Wave 2-5 acknowledgement to this final plan (so the two parallel Wave 3 plans, 03-04 and 03-05, could not collide writing to this file).

1. **Plan 03-03 (Wave 2 tracer — the 24-hour realizable lead end-to-end).** Built `fetch_previous_runs` under its own `PREVIOUS_RUNS_MODEL_ID = "gfs_seamless"` pin (live-confirmed distinct from the legacy `FORECAST_MODEL_ID = "ecmwf_ifs"`, whose Previous-Runs archive returns all-null for nearly the entire candidate window); measured — rather than trusted from Open-Meteo's or D-04's own "exact by construction" framing — that `previous_day1`'s effective lead is `24 + (hour % 6)` hours, exact only at `hour % 6 == 0`; generalized the anchored-pair-frame contract (`anchored_pair_frame`/`assert_exact_lead`/`filter_to_exact_lead`) by a required keyword-only `lead_hours` parameter (a checkpoint, resolved by auto-selecting the plan's own recommended option, since a defaulted lead-discriminating parameter is the exact invisible-failure class that had already derailed this phase twice); and ran a live one-city validation-split tracer showing `raw_forecast` (MAE 131.461) genuinely beating `persistence_24h` (MAE 165.426) — the in-repo refutation of the bit-identity artifact that forced the pivot.
2. **Plan 03-04 (Wave 3, parallel with 03-05 — the four non-fitted arms and corridor-level scoring).** Built `NON_FITTED_ARMS` (persistence_24h, nwp_wind_delta, raw_forecast, and the oracle under `ORACLE_ARM_NAME`) as one factory registry; promoted scoring from single-city to corridor level via `forecast_training.corridor_weakest_link` applied independently per column group (truth, anchor, and each arm's own prediction) at its own time; and made the oracle's isolation from the candidate mapping structural (`corridor_experiment_report` unconditionally pops it into its own key) rather than a caller-remembered label. A live two-corridor run measured the oracle's ceiling over `raw_forecast` at roughly 98 amps (AUSTIN_CORPUS_CHRISTI) and 148 amps (MIDLAND_EL_PASO) — the headroom every later correction arm would be judged against.
3. **Plan 03-05 (Wave 3, parallel with 03-04 — the two ML correction families and the arm-5 horizon seam).** Built `backend/forecast_correction_models.py` as a standalone module: u/v-vector-space wind correction (both pooling strategies) and convective-term correction through the existing `q_conv_override` seam (both target parameterizations), each with a validation-only selector that reports the full score table, not just the winner. Added an additive, default-preserving `horizon_minutes` parameter to `forecast_training.build_training_matrix`, gated by an exact-equality (`np.array_equal`) golden-fixture capture taken from the unmodified source before the edit — unblocking arm 5's 24-hour target shift without mutating a global config constant. Corrected the plan's own illustrative row-count claim ("24 fewer rows" → verified "23 fewer") after hand-deriving the actual relationship, rather than trusting the plan text as written.
4. **Plan 03-06 (Wave 4 — the Expectation commit, the leakage probe, and the single held-out test-set run).** Committed the decision record's Expectation section in the same isolated two-file commit that unlocked the held-out test split (`4d964cf`), predicting `raw_forecast` would win narrowly and that the refit reference model would underperform persistence as Phase 1's 60-minute result had. Wired arms 3/4/5 into a seven-entry `ALL_ARMS` registry and built a leakage probe with demonstrated power against a deliberately sabotaged arm. After a checkpoint whose three preconditions (isolated Expectation commit, a power-tested leakage probe, and fixed, previously-validation-selected arm configurations) were independently re-verified and explicit human authorization was given, ran the test set exactly once (`--lead-hours 24 --split test`, both corridors, 786 rows each, live network) and transcribed the result verbatim — including the finding, stated without editorializing, that it contradicted the pre-registered Expectation on two specific points (the predicted winner, and the refit model's predicted loss).
5. **Plan 03-07 (Wave 5 — the completed decision record and the phase's closing records; this plan).** Wrote the record's Verdict, Limitations, Open Questions, and References sections, reading the measured results against the Expectation exactly as it was committed and never editing it: named `ml_cooling_corrected` as the winner at both corridors, stated plainly where the Expectation's specific predictions were wrong (the predicted winner did not win; the refit reference model reversed from Phase 1's loss to a win) and the most likely reasoning error behind each, and separated statistical distinguishability from the amperes-scale practical-significance threshold the Expectation had committed to before the test set was read — several winning margins cleared the statistical bar but not the practical one. Added a clearly-separated, explicitly non-primary Legacy 1-Hour Arm section with its bit-identity caveat placed before any score it quotes. Closed `ROADMAP.md`'s Phase 3 section (replacing the "superseded, to be replanned" placeholder with the actual five-wave structure, all seven plans checked, and a disposition line under each success criterion) and `03-VALIDATION.md` (every task row flipped from pending to green, both Wave 0 checklists closed, `nyquist_compliant: true`) — scoped edits verified to touch no other phase's `ROADMAP.md` entry and no `backend/` file.

No deviation across these five plans required a Rule 4 (architectural) escalation; auto-fixed issues (a `main()` report-argument inconsistency in Plan 03-03, and the row-count arithmetic correction in Plan 03-05) are documented in each plan's own `SUMMARY.md`. The full suite's failed count (23, all in the pre-existing `backend/tests/corridor_discretization_test.py`, logged in `.planning/WINDOWS.md`) was unchanged across all five plans — no regression introduced anywhere in Waves 2-5. Each plan happened on its own assigned isolated worktree branch, closing with a `docs(0N-0M)` commit for its own `SUMMARY.md`; per this phase's worktree-executor convention, `.planning/workstreams/ml-modeling/STATE.md` and `ROADMAP.md` were left untouched by Plans 03-03 through 03-06 — the orchestrator (for STATE.md) and this final plan (for ROADMAP.md, per its own explicit file scope) own those writes.

This makes the two assertions mutually unsatisfiable for any document containing `### ` subsections — which Task 2's own inner split explicitly requires to exist. Did not modify PLAN.md (out of this plan's file scope); instead independently confirmed the document's actual substantive correctness (citation counts, subsection counts, table presence, required language) using the evidently-intended outer boundary `r'^## '` (which does not match `### `), documented the isolated regex proof in each task's own commit message, and logged the finding to `.planning/WINDOWS.md` (kind: deviation, phase 04).

### Session: 2026-09-30 through 2026-10-01, conducted by Aaron Phan — `ml-modeling` workstream Phase 4 (Forecast Correction Follow-up Audit) closing session: consolidated disclosure for all six plans (04-01 through 04-06, GSD, isolated worktrees)

Claude Code was used, via the GSD (`/gsd-execute-phase` and `/gsd-plan-phase`) toolset running as isolated-worktree executor agents across six separate plan executions (plus one orchestrator-driven planning session), to conduct an adversarial, independent follow-up audit of Phase 3's already-frozen decision record (`docs/data-science/decisions/forecast_correction_experiment.md`) and to close out Phase 4 of the `ml-modeling` workstream. This is the one consolidated Phase 4 entry this file's own convention calls for, since several individual plans' own sessions (04-01, 04-03, and 04-06, this entry's own plan) deferred their ACKNOWLEDGEMENTS.md update to a closing session rather than writing a separate entry per plan, matching the precedent Phase 3's own Wave 2-5 consolidated entry set. Walking all six plans:

1. **Plan 04-01 (the audit spine and the first real finding).** Built `assert_basis_excludes_frozen_test_rows` — a mechanical guard raising on any frame carrying a timestamp inside Phase 3's held-out test window — and three named, code-enforced audit bases (`SYNTHETIC`/`ABLATION`/`FRESH`), each with a written may-say/may-not-say statement. Delivered the phase's first real, code-grounded finding: Phase 3's entire "truth" side is a physics-model reanalysis output (ERA5/ERA5-Land via Open-Meteo's Archive API), never a sensor measurement, confirmed by a direct `grep` showing the frozen record never states this. Independently re-derived the frozen split boundary (matched the pinned 2026-03-17 exactly for both corridors, with a 2-row-per-corridor sub-day pin-safety clip discovered and documented). Measured the real ABLATION (784 validation rows/corridor) and FRESH (0 rows/corridor) bases at run time — FRESH measured zero because "yesterday UTC" resolved to the frozen window's own last day, the expected honest answer the phase's own research anticipated, not a defect. A human checkpoint reviewed all four measured numbers before any downstream plan built on them.
2. **Plan 04-02 (D-01's five bullets, closed by measurement).** Established that the lead-time calibration gate proves an archive-to-archive value match, never real-time dissemination availability — a claim-type limitation distinct from the frozen record's already-disclosed coverage limitation. Built an exhaustive time-reference partition proving every column of both assembled frame shapes resolves to exactly one of two clock instants. Re-ran the leakage probe on real ABLATION rows in **both callable shapes**: Mode A (the exact column-reader shape the frozen test-set run actually used) reported zero arms changed, oracle included — proving the frozen run's own callable shape was structurally powerless to catch a leak, not that no leak existed; Mode B (live-recomputing callables plus a deliberately sabotaged arm, run on all four project cities) demonstrated genuine power, with the oracle and the sabotaged arm both changing and all six real candidates unchanged, for both corridors with zero exceptions — closing the "only demonstrated on synthetic data" gap with a real-data re-run. Measured arm 5's feature-to-label offset at exactly 24.0 hours and the split boundaries at zero shared timestamps, both from returned indices rather than from the parameters under audit.
3. **Plan 04-03 (the strengthened comparison, D-02).** Built a paired block bootstrap proven to reduce exactly to the harness's own i.i.d. method at block length one, swept across block lengths 1/4/8/28. Measured the real ABLATION-basis out-of-sample comparison (fitted arms refit on TRAIN rows alone, a genuinely held-out re-run for this phase's own purposes) and found AUSTIN_CORPUS_CHRISTI replicates the frozen verdict cleanly, but MIDLAND_EL_PASO does **not** — a different candidate (`ml_wind_corrected`) now leads, and `ml_cooling_corrected`'s margin over the refit model is statistically indistinguishable from zero where the frozen record reported a distinguishable edge. A human checkpoint reviewed the measured numbers before the write-up and approved the strongest honest framing ("contradicted," not "unresolved") for Midland. Formally reserved a fresh chronological period (starting 2026-09-30, minimum 56 rows / 14 days) for any future confirmation claim.
4. **Plan 04-04 (the conservative-rating analysis, D-03 — a genuinely new axis Phase 3 never measured).** Measured the overrating profile (frequency, magnitude, consecutive-row run length) for every real candidate at both corridors, finding overrating concentrates at 100% in the lowest-wind bin — exactly at the conductor's own measured natural-vs-forced convection crossover (0.1428 m/s at 35.0°C) — versus 80.2%/78.9% immediately above it, a real physical pattern rather than numerical instability. Built a three-period (fit/calibrate/evaluate) lower-ampacity quantile pipeline via scikit-learn's own pinball loss, with calibration improving the evaluate-period exceedance rate for every (candidate, alpha) pair except one, named explicitly rather than hidden. At a matched 5% overrating rate, `ml_cooling_corrected` retained the most usable capacity of any real candidate at both corridors — the section's own headline finding.
5. **Plan 04-05 (D-04's corrections, measured rather than asserted).** Measured, by sweeping a wind grid across the convection crossover, that the low-wind ampacity response is finite, monotone, and continuous — a physics floor, not a numerical defect — for both corridors. Independently re-verified Phase 3's Success-Criterion-4 convective-seam bit-identity claim from a fresh code path against the committed golden fixture (never invoking the existing equality test itself), and answered D-04's sub-floor routing question by attempting it directly: a positive, below-the-natural-convection-floor corrected term **is** permitted to reach the physics through the `q_conv_override` seam, a real finding about the seam's exposure. Appended a six-item, append-only amendment to the frozen record, under two structural guarantees verified **before** the commit, not discovered as a gate failure afterward: the `## Expectation` and `## Measured results` sections were extracted from the committed file and the working copy and compared **byte-for-byte**, proven identical; and every line of the committed file was confirmed to still appear, in order, in the amended one (246 lines added, zero deleted or reworded). Published this record's own corrected verdict: keep both `ml_cooling_corrected` and `reference_ml_refit_24h` live at low-to-moderate confidence, naming exactly what would strengthen or weaken that confidence.
6. **Plan 04-06 (D-05's integration-readiness plan — this closing plan).** Answered the frozen record's own Open Question — why the refit reference model reversed from a loss at 60 minutes to a win at 24 hours — with a real, out-of-sample feature ablation: three variants of the same estimator family (all features, lags only, covariates only), differing only in a provably disjoint, dropped-not-zeroed column subset, fit on the real ABLATION train rows and scored on the real ABLATION validation rows. The result is corridor-dependent: the ablation **supports** the frozen record's own proposed explanation (that the non-lag weather covariates carry the skill the lag features lose as they go stale) at AUSTIN_CORPUS_CHRISTI, but **does not** at MIDLAND_EL_PASO — a finding reported honestly rather than smoothed into one verdict. The cooling-rediscovery hypothesis test measured residual correlations of 0.846 (Austin) and 0.803 (Midland) between the covariates-only variant and `ml_cooling_corrected`'s own residuals (reconstructed from Plan 04-03's persisted residuals, never re-scored), reported as consistent with — but not proof of — an implicitly rediscovered cooling-like correction, with the prediction-versus-residual distinction stated explicitly since two models can share a diurnal signal without sharing a mechanism. Wrote the field-level minimum data contract (19 named fields: archived forecasts, three distinct timestamps, local observations with quality flags, units, sensor siting including measurement height above ground, and conductor/span metadata), each field traced to the specific audit finding that motivates it — the `availability_timestamp` field closes D-01 Finding 4's own central gap (no archive-to-archive comparison can ever evidence real-time dissemination). Split every one of this audit's own recommendations into exactly one of two tables: six the ML track can complete now, six genuinely blocked on the new data contract. Outlined — never built — packaging, runtime forecast fetching, stale-data handling, a four-rung fallback ladder ending at the corridor's static rating with no optimistic guess at any rung (mirroring `DLRSafetyMonitor`'s own G6 `except Exception` resolution), shadow evaluation, monitoring (naming the overrating rate explicitly), and rollback. Named the 60-minute-vs-24-hour-vs-both product question as unresolved and assigned it to whoever owns product scope, recommending no blanket replacement. Closed the ROADMAP's Phase 4 entry with a disposition under each of its five success criteria — four fully MET, one (the comparison-strengthening criterion) MET IN PART, since the fresh-chronological-holdout reservation is a reservation, not an executed confirmation, until calendar time accrues.

**What this closing session verified structurally, across all six plans.** The frozen record's own `## Expectation` and `## Measured results` sections were proven byte-identical to their committed versions after Plan 04-05's amendment — not asserted, extracted and compared byte-for-byte, with the append-only discipline independently confirmed (every originally-committed line still present, in order). The leakage probe was re-run on real data in **two distinct callable shapes** (Plan 04-02): the shape the frozen test-set run actually used (Mode A, structurally powerless) and a live-recomputing shape with a deliberately sabotaged arm (Mode B, demonstrated genuine power) — a finding that changes what evidence the frozen record can cite for its own Success Criterion 3, without changing the underlying conclusion that none of the seven real arms leaks. The fresh-basis row count was **measured, not assumed**, at zero for both corridors as of this audit's run date (2026-09-30) and reconfirmed at zero at this closing session's own run date — the basis accrues four corridor rows per calendar day and reaches the reserved 56-row/14-day minimum roughly two weeks after the audit's original run date, a fact this closing session's own ROADMAP disposition states explicitly as outstanding rather than silently treating the reservation as equivalent to a completed confirmation. And no claim anywhere in this phase's six plans was ever upgraded from an ABLATION-basis replication to a FRESH-basis confirmation — the Corrected verdict's own confidence statement says this in as many words ("not a confirmation — no claim in this record is a confirmation on a genuinely fresh chronological period"), and this closing session found no place across any of the six plans' summaries where that discipline was relaxed.

**Deviations, classified.** Across the six plans: one Rule 1 bug (Plan 04-02's `Timedelta` string-formatting defect, hiding the "24" hour-count substring a verify script checked for literally); two Rule 3 blocking fixes (Plan 04-02's reimplementation of the val-side extraction for a train+val-only stacked frame that the harness's own three-way splitter cannot accept; Plan 04-04's flaky-test fix for a hash-randomized RNG seed); one Rule 1 bug in Plan 04-04's test suite (the same hash-seeding class, independently found and fixed); two Rule 1 bugs in Plan 04-05's own doc-verify literal-substring matching (reworded, not substantively changed); and a recurring, project-wide **verify-script defect class** — a self-contradicting `^##+ ` section-boundary regex that also matches the `^###+ ` subsection markers the same scripts then require to exist, logged across four separate `.planning/WINDOWS.md` entries (ids 2, 3, and this session's own id 4 for Plan 04-06's `D05_SECTION_OK` script) rather than silently worked around with no record. In every instance, no PLAN.md file was edited (out of each plan's own file scope); each plan's own substantive correctness was instead independently re-verified using the evidently-intended corrected boundary regex, with the proof (word counts, subsection counts, table counts) recorded in that plan's own commit message or SUMMARY.md. No deviation in this phase required a Rule 4 (architectural) escalation.

**Commits, branches, and what was left to the orchestrator.** Each of the six plans executed on its own assigned isolated worktree branch (`worktree-agent-<id>`), closing with per-task `feat`/`docs`/`fix` commits followed by a `docs` commit for that plan's own `SUMMARY.md`. Per this phase's worktree-executor convention, `.planning/workstreams/ml-modeling/STATE.md` was left untouched throughout and is owned by the orchestrator; `ROADMAP.md` was likewise left untouched by Plans 04-01 through 04-05 and closed by this plan (04-06) alone, per this plan's own explicit file-scope exception to that convention, with every other phase's ROADMAP entry and the Overview/Backlog/Execution-Order sections verified byte-identical to their committed versions before this entry's own commit.

fixed — none taken on the reporting user's word alone:**

1. A reversed RMSE claim in the "Both error metrics" subsection: the document said RMSE "still favors

   lower than refit's 165.596, so RMSE actually favors cooling there, reversing (not reproducing) the
   frozen record's own MAE/RMSE split for this pair. Cross-checked directly against
   `ablation_basis_report`'s own `mae_rmse_ordering_disagreements` field, which names the real
   disagreeing pair as `persistence_24h` vs. `raw_forecast`, not cooling vs. refit. The stronger, actually
   correct finding — `ml_wind_corrected` beats BOTH cooling and refit on BOTH metrics simultaneously at
   this corridor — was substituted throughout (main prose, both disposition tables, the Corrected
   verdict's headline recommendation and its findings-by-decision list).
2. An undercounted pairwise claim: "cooling's margin excludes zero only once" (vs. refit at Austin) —
   the document's own table already showed a second exclusion (vs. `raw_forecast` at MIDLAND_EL_PASO,

3. An overclaim that the block-length-sensitivity finding (on the `ml_wind_corrected` vs.
   `reference_ml_refit_24h` margin) proves the frozen record's own published margins were "optimistic" —
   softened throughout: that margin is one the frozen record never computed, on a different sample than

   method's sensitivity to serial correlation, not a direct re-test of any margin the frozen record
   actually reported. A direct re-test of the frozen record's own margins through the same block

**Checked and found NOT to be bugs, with the reasoning made explicit in the document rather than left
implicit** (no code or document correction made beyond adding the reasoning itself):

  traced against the actual measured timestamps and the standard static-fit-then-score evaluation
  protocol this project already uses elsewhere — not a leak, because the correct and already-checked
  guard (train's max VALID time precedes every scored row's own VALID time) is sufficient for a model
  that is frozen at its training cutoff and never refit per row; the training cutoff postdating one
  row's own decision time (a quantity 24 hours earlier than that row's valid time) does not by itself
  constitute a leak under that protocol. Added an explicit paragraph to Finding 7 making this reasoning
  visible rather than leaving the apparent inconsistency unaddressed.
- The two-stage quantile pipeline's leakage discipline (`fit_lower_quantile_model` / 
  `quantile_calibration_report`): read directly — stage 1 asserts, by name and by construction, that no
  `truth_*` column enters its own feature set; the stage-2 calibration offset is learned on the Calibrate
  period only and applied unchanged on Evaluate. No leak found; no document change needed here.
- Boundary-gap (pin-safety clip) consistency across all fitted models: grepped every fit/score call site
  across the module (`build_basis_manifest`, `ablation_basis_report`, the quantile pipeline's own
  three-period split) and confirmed the same `< pinned_boundary` clip is applied at every one, not
  selectively.
- 60-minute vs. 24-hour claim separation: grepped the full document for every mention of either lead;
  all are already kept explicitly distinct, with no summary table or verdict sentence conflating them.

**A new safety-relevant finding added, not requested verbatim but directly responsive to the
integration-assumptions question about upward clipping:** read `predict_cooling_correction`
(`backend/forecast_correction_models.py:505-521`, read-only) and `ieee738_ampacity`'s heat-balance line
(`backend/thermal_model.py:255-257`, read-only) directly. Confirmed: the correction model's own guard
rejects only a non-positive corrected convective term; nothing clamps the term on the HIGH side before it
enters the heat balance, which itself only ever floors at zero. An overcorrection that predicts too high a
term would inflate reported ampacity with no code-level ceiling to catch it — added as an explicit
safety-relevant finding to the document's D-04/D-05 discussion, since the cooling correction is a fitted
statistical residual adjustment, not a physically-derived constraint, and nothing guarantees its output
stays within a physically defensible range. No production code was touched; this is a documented finding
for a future integration phase to weigh, not a fix made here.

`forecast_correction_experiment.md`'s own structural gates against the real pre-Phase-4 base commit

the prior pass) after the fix and confirmed it still passes on real data.

### Session: 2026-10-05 (continued), conducted by Aaron Phan — building the deferred ABLATION/FRESH basis-selection gate (direct working-tree task, no worktree isolation)

The prior pass deliberately deferred the ABLATION/FRESH basis-selection parameter rather than build
it under time pressure. This session built it, carefully, in `backend/forecast_experiment_audit.py`:

1. `basis_evaluation_report(corridor, *, basis="ABLATION", confirm_fresh_run=False, ...)` — the one
   shared evaluation entry point. `basis="ABLATION"` is an unconditional pass-through to the
   existing, already-audited `ablation_basis_report` (zero behavior change). `basis="FRESH"` raises
   immediately, before touching any FRESH-basis data or even the manifest file, unless the caller
   passes `confirm_fresh_run=True` explicitly — proven by a test that monkeypatches
   `build_basis_manifest` to assert it is never called on the unflagged path.
2. `_fresh_confirmation_report` — only reachable through the gate above. Refits wind/cooling/arm-5
   on ABLATION-TRAIN alone (the frozen protocol's own rule: FRESH is confirmation-only, never a
   training source), then scores against the real FRESH-basis frame. Enforces, in order: the
   frozen-window guard under `basis="FRESH"`, a decision-time boundary assertion, a minimum-sample
   gate (`CONFIRMATION_MINIMUM_ROWS = 590`, the protocol's own derived target) that refuses to score
   an under-powered sample even with `confirm_fresh_run=True` — that flag grants permission to
   attempt a read, not permission to skip the sample-size requirement — and the same Mode-B leakage
   probe (with its own positive controls) `ablation_basis_report` already uses, re-verified against
   this run's own fitted objects and FRESH-dated frame.
3. Closed a pre-existing, previously-unflagged gap in `fresh_basis_attempt` itself: before this
   session, any ordinary call to `ablation_basis_report` (which calls `fresh_basis_attempt`
   internally just to attach row-count metadata) would have started silently scoring real FRESH
   outcomes the moment the FRESH row count crossed that function's own 56-row smoke-test floor —
   with no flag at all. Added the same `confirm_fresh_run` gate there; without it, reaching the
   smoke-test floor now returns a `"confirmation_capable_unconfirmed"` branch with no scoring,
   rather than proceeding.
4. Six new tests, all on SYNTHETIC fixtures dated after `FROZEN_WINDOW_END` (never real FRESH data):
   the gate rejecting an unknown basis, rejecting `confirm_fresh_run=True` paired with
   `basis="ABLATION"` (a likely caller mistake), the unflagged-FRESH-raises-before-touching-data
   proof, the minimum-row refusal, the ABLATION pass-through's shape parity, and one full
   end-to-end positive-path run through the gated FRESH pipeline (required a date-range-aware
   fake for `anchored_pair_frame` and a feature-frame fix so arm 5's lag features can see FRESH-
   dated history; a handful of leading synthetic rows are dropped for lag warm-up and the count is
   reported in the output, consistent with this phase's existing "honest absence" discipline rather
   than imputing or raising).
5. Did NOT complete, left open per this pass's own time allocation: extending the leakage probe
   into the quantile pipeline's own internal stages (item 2 of the prior pass's directive); a full

   (item 3, partial -- see next entry for what WAS done on item 3's "exact numbers" ask).

## Keeping This Updated

This file should be reviewed and updated to reflect new AI-assisted work **before any significant commit** — i.e., before committing new features, substantial documentation, or non-trivial refactors produced with AI assistance. A commit is "significant" for this purpose if it would need describing in a standup/status update; trivial fixes (typos, formatting) don't require an update here.

1. Added `fetch_live_anchor_observation` (`backend/forecast_shadow_pipeline.py`): fetches a real, near-real-time station observation via `weather_provider.fetch_weather`'s Synoptic path — the production multi-provider observation composer — never the Open-Meteo Archive API reanalysis path training used (that archive lags ~1 day and cannot serve a live "most recent observation" request).
2. Added `attach_wind_correction_inputs`, and wired `WindCorrectionShadowMethod` to call it internally from inside its own `predict()` (when `endpoint_city`/`valid_time`/`decision_time` are supplied) — so a missing/failed anchor source is caught by `ShadowPipeline.predict`'s existing fail-safe fallback, with a real `fallback_reason`, instead of crashing the caller.
